How Perth Easily Became Australia's Gateway To Europe

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Australian travelers bound for Europe have always had to face a two-stage journey, with a domestic transcontinental flight to an east-coast hub, followed by a long-haul connection through the Middle East or Southeast Asia. There is one location that bucks this trend, that being Perth. Sitting nearly 2,000 miles (3,218 km) closer to London than Sydney, quietly positioned Western Australia as the nation's natural international springboard, the Western Australian city has become the main entry point for non-stop flights to continental Europe.

That transformation did not happen by accident; it came as a result of a fundamental realignment of airline fleet strategies, airport infrastructure investments, and international route rights. Using advanced long-range twin-engine aircraft, Qantas turned Perth International Airport (PER) into a high-yield international hub. But as airspace disruptions and competing long-haul technologies emerge, how long can Western Australia maintain its exclusive hold as Australia's direct bridge to Europe?

Perth became Australia’s gateway to Europe due to its location, as the vast distance between the southern continent and European airspace is within the maximum range limits of modern twin-engine airliners. In March 2018, Qantas launched its historic 9,010-mile (14,499 km) Perth to London Heathrow Airport(LHR) route using a 236-seat Boeing 787-9, as covered by Fortune. Flying directly out of Western Australia cuts three to four hours off the total travel time compared to departing from Sydney or Melbourne Airport(MEL), which therefore removes the traditional intermediate stopover in Singapore Changi Airport (SIN), Dubai International Airport(DXB), or Doha Hamad International Airport(DOH).

According to reporting from The Points Guy, that single route showed that non-stop ultra-long-haul flights between Australia and Europe were not only technically feasible, but exceptionally profitable. The success of the London service encouraged Qantas and local state planners to expand the Perth hub, eventually leading to the launch of Perth– Rome Fiumicino Airport(FCO) in June 2022, the first direct flight connecting Australia with continental Europe, and a subsequent seasonal non-stop service to Paris Charles De Gaulle Airport(CDG).

As highlighted in Travel Weekly, Qantas confirmed that 2026 would be its biggest year yet for Western Australia, adding nearly 300,000 seats between Perth and London, Paris, and Rome. In fact, the Perth–Rome route alone gained 44 extra flights and close to 10,000 more inbound seats between May and October 2026, as per the Government Of Western Australia. This massive expansion reinforced Western Australia's unique status as the only Australian state offering direct, non-stop passenger services into the European continent.

Supporting high-yield international transit traffic meant Perth Airport and Qantas had to overcome the separation between domestic and international terminals. Traditionally, passengers connecting from Melbourne, Sydney, or Brisbane had to land at the domestic terminal precinct and transfer across the airfield to the international terminal, creating a friction-filled connection experience. To resolve this, Qantas negotiated a custom arrangement to process international customs and immigration directly within its domestic Terminal 3 and 4 wing, as reported by MTA Travel.

The temporary operational fix laid the groundwork for Perth Airport’s massive "One Airport" consolidation program. According to an official media statement from the Government of Western Australia, WA Premier Roger Cook stated that these expanded international connections "cement WA's status as the gateway to Australia from Europe", noting that over 20 non-stop international routes now operate out of Perth. This multi-billion-dollar infrastructure effort aims to consolidate all domestic and international flights into a unified central terminal precinct, drastically reducing transfer friction for connecting passengers.

As well as the European routes, Qantas expanded Perth's network horizon even further by adding non-stop flights from Perth to Johannesburg Airport(JNB) and Auckland Airport(AKL) starting in December 2025. Connecting trans-Tasman, African, and European corridors through a single hub meant that Perth Airport could change its identity from a regional destination into a major global transit nexus. However, just as Perth was solidifying its status as an unshakeable aviation crossroads, major geopolitical shifts halfway across the globe put its route network to a tough operational test.

A closer look at why Qantas made adjustment to one of the world's longest non-stop flights

Geopolitical conflicts across the Middle East have caused major challenges for global air corridors, forcing international airlines to navigate closed or restricted airspace over Iran, Iraq, and the Levant region. For traditional Gulf mega-carriers operating out of hubs like Dubai, Doha, and Abu Dhabi, flight paths faced rerouting or temporary operational halts. As reported by Aerospace Global News, this systemic disruption created an unprecedented surge in demand for alternative routes linking Australia directly to Europe. Travelers who previously opted for a Middle East transfer sought out Qantas's direct services out of Western Australia to avoid the volatile airspace altogether.

However, the unforgiving flight-planning realities of ultra-long-haul aviation meant that Qantas faced its own operational complications. As highlighted in coverage by Simple Flying, westbound headwinds combined with strict airspace avoidance protocols forced Qantas to adjust its flagship outbound flights. Outbound services from Perth to London and Sydney to Paris via Perth were rerouted through Singapore Changi for technical refueling stops, though the inbound flights from London and Paris back to Perth continued to operate non-stop.

During these operational changes, Perth–Rome stood out as a critical non-stop survivor. In order to capture redirected European demand, Qantas boosted Perth–Rome services from four weekly flights to daily operations, adding over 1,400 seats per week to the continent during peak seasons. Such tactical network shifts brought to center stage both the strategic resilience and the delicate operational vulnerability of running ultra-long-haul corridors out of Western Australia.

Operating commercial non-stop sectors exceeding 8,000 miles (12,874 km) is exactly where operational factors need to be monitored closely. Payload capacity, fuel burn, and takeoff weight limits massively affect the viability of the flight, and so a balance needs to be achieved. The 787-9 aircraft assigned to Qantas’s long-haul Perth operations feature a custom passenger configuration of just 236 seats, significantly lower than the standard high-density layouts deployed by international competitors. Restricting cabin capacity reduces total payload weight, allowing the twin-engine widebody to carry the massive fuel load required for a 17-hour block time.

Every additional seat, cargo pallet, or gallon of jet fuel alters the aircraft's takeoff performance, particularly during warm Australian summer months. When strong winter jet streams push against westbound aircraft over the Indian Ocean, flight planning managers rigorously weigh passenger counts against fuel reserves. If headwind components exceed baseline estimates, the aircraft risks exceeding its Maximum Takeoff Weight (MTOW) or infringing strict Extended-range Twin-engine Operational Performance Standards (ETOPS) margins.

The constraints here explain why Qantas relies on premium-heavy cabin configurations and elevated ticket yields to make these non-stop routes viable. Despite this, as airframe technologies advance, Perth's hard-won geographic monopoly faces a threat from within Qantas’s own future fleet commitments.

Qantas shakes up its international network to offer more flights to Europe via Singapore.

The long-term outlook for Perth as Australia's main European gateway faces a major test from Qantas's flagship ultra-long-haul initiative, Project Sunrise. Designed to connect Sydney and Melbourne directly to London Heathrow and New York non-stop using ultra-long-range Airbus A350-1000ULR aircraft, Project Sunrise will completely remove the need for an intermediate domestic transit stop in Western Australia.

Once direct non-stop flights from the East Coast launch, a significant portion of high-yield corporate travelers who currently route through Perth will opt for point-to-point non-stop services from Sydney or Melbourne. According to reporting by The Points Guy, Project Sunrise specifically targets high-yield travelers on Australia's busiest transcontinental corridors, potentially diverting transit traffic away from Western Australia’s hub.

Replacing Perth is not quite the plan, as Qantas plans to position the two operations as complementary long-haul networks. While Project Sunrise caters to premium demand from Australia's largest financial centers on the east coast, Perth will continue serving as a regional springboard, capturing origin-and-destination demand across Western Australia, trans-Tasman feeder traffic, and routes to Southern Africa.

Perth's transformation into Australia's primary direct gateway to Europe is really something special, something that quite often gets overlooked in the global aviation world. Non-stop ultra-long-haul routes could succeed using fuel-efficient twin-engine widebodies, so Qantas and Perth Airport were able to jointly morph international travel patterns across the Southern Hemisphere.

The long-term test for Western Australia will be maintaining its route volume as Project Sunrise aircraft enter service and direct east-coast routes come online. Continued investment in terminal consolidation under Perth Airport’s "One Airport" masterplan, combined with scheduled route restorations, including Qantas returning the Perth–London sector to a full non-stop rotation, are really what will determine whether Perth retains its dominance as a transit hub.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

Airspace restrictions and jet fuel economics are still highly volatile, but Western Australia's geographic location is an unshakable natural asset. Perth was able to make direct connections from Australia to the other side of the world possible, so even if Project Sunrise takes away its lead, we still have Perth to thank for opening up one of the longest direct route selections in the world.

Chainalysis Discloses Separate October 30 Security Incident Involving User Data

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainalysis disclosed a security incident on October 30 affecting a subset of customer-related information, including names, email addresses and certain user or role labels, according to the company. “No evidence of compromise” is not proof that a particular system was mathematically impossible to access.

The apparent boundary between identity information and investigative data is the central fact in this disclosure. Readers should not dismiss exposed contact details as harmless, but neither should they infer a breach of every product the company operates. Precision is particularly important when the firm itself works in cybercrime investigations.

Chainalysis disclosed a security incident on October 30 affecting a subset of customer-related information, including names, email addresses and certain user or role labels, according to the company. It said there was no evidence that its investigative graph data, credentials or user sessions were compromised. This was a separate incident and should not be merged with Chainalysis’ analysis of the Binance theft. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Businesses that help investigate blockchain crime may also hold conventional personal and organisational information about their customers. Even when a breach does not expose transaction intelligence, names and roles can be useful to attackers crafting phishing or social-engineering messages. That risk depends on the exact data exposed and how those records can be combined with information already public.

The disclosure underscores the value of keeping customer identity systems, investigation data and access credentials in well-defined security boundaries. A malicious actor with one type of information should not automatically gain another. The company’s description can be used to understand the stated scope, but good incident response also includes validating those claims over time.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

“No evidence of compromise” is not proof that a particular system was mathematically impossible to access. It is a statement about what investigators had found as of the company’s update. Affected customers should rely on verified notices and avoid clicking unsolicited reset links. Equally, the existence of a data event does not prove that all users’ credentials were stolen.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Next, look for the company’s follow-up findings, notice procedures and any confirmed misuse of exposed information. For organisations using investigative vendors, the broader lesson is that sensitive workflows should limit data sharing and use strong independent authentication. Vendor risk exists even when the core analytical product is technically sound.

NBCUniversal, Hornbill Capital Back EloElo, Parent of Indian Microdrama Platform Story TV, in $80 Million Funding Round

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

NBCUniversal and India-focused investment firm Hornbill Capital have co-led an $80 million Series C round in EloElo Group, the Bengaluru-based company behind Indian microdrama platform Story TV.

MIT Investment Co. joined the round alongside existing backers Play Ventures, Kalaari Capital, Waterbridge Ventures and Griffin Gaming Partners, among others. The raise takes EloElo’s total funding to $130 million. Avendus Capital served as sole financial advisor on the deal. Related Stories ‘Father’s House,’ Starring David Dencik and Trine-Dyrholm, Picked Up by DR Sales Ahead of Tallinn Competition

EloElo was founded in 2020 by CEO Saurabh Pandey, a former Flipkart executive.

“At NBCUniversal, we see significant opportunity in mobile-first storytelling and emerging formats like microdramas to engage audiences and meet evolving viewing habits. Under Saurabh’s leadership, EloElo has developed a differentiated, mobile-first approach with a clear focus on content and storytelling, positioning the company for long-term success. We’re excited to bring NBCUniversal’s global capabilities to the partnership as EloElo expands beyond India, including into the U.S. market,” said Deepak Arya, executive VP of corporate strategy and development at NBCUniversal.

Story TV now carries more than 5,000 microdramas in six languages and adds a new title every three hours, with each user spending 95 minutes a day on the app on average.

“A billion people now reach for the phone before any other screen, and the entertainment built for them will be video first, in their own language, in episodes that fit their day,” Pandey said.

Alongside Story TV, the group operates micro learning app Master, AI companion agent Voxy AI and Dolphin AI, an agentic AI creative suite it introduced recently at the FICCI Frames conference. The group’s apps have more than 40 million monthly active users combined and have passed 300 million installs, and EloElo said its revenue multiplied over the past year.

The company will put the new money toward original programming in local languages, an overseas launch for its microdramas and further development of Dolphin AI, among other projects.

“Story TV shows us what India wants to watch, and Dolphin AI gives us the economics to make it, which is how we have scaled with solid unit economics,” Pandey said.

Hornbill Capital, set up in 2014 by Manoj Thakur, has previously invested in PhysicsWallah, KreditBee, Dhan and Nazara Technologies.

“EloElo Group combines strong creative capabilities with the ability to scale content through technology, which is something we deeply believe in,” said Thakur, the firm’s founder and managing partner.

Research firm Omdia projects the global microdrama business will generate $14 billion before 2026 is out. Redseer estimates that 40 million Indians made at least one microdrama purchase during fiscal 2026, while a report from Lumikai forecasts the local market will hit $4.5 billion by 2030.

Hurdle hints and answers for November 4, 2026

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

If you like playing daily word games like Wordle, then Hurdle is a great game to add to your routine.

There are five rounds to the game. The first round sees you trying to guess the word, with correct, misplaced, and incorrect letters shown in each guess. If you guess the correct answer, it'll take you to the next hurdle, providing the answer to the last hurdle as your first guess. This can give you several clues or none, depending on the words. For the final hurdle, every correct answer from previous hurdles is shown, with correct and misplaced letters clearly shown.

An important note is that the number of times a letter is highlighted from previous guesses does necessarily indicate the number of times that letter appears in the final hurdle.

If you find yourself stuck at any step of today's Hurdle, don't worry! We have you covered.

How To Keep Your Middle Class Membership Card

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The middle class is the best class in the world. It is the largest class, which means it’s the most important class. Politicians pander to us by saying nice things and giving us all sorts of random benefits, like the child tax credit or stimulus checks if you make a middle-class income.

But once you get to the upper middle class, or the mass affluent class, those government benefits start going away. You make more than the middle class, but not enough more to avoid the sting of higher taxes and optional lifestyle choices, like paying for private school. And because you make more than the middle class, you are sometimes vilified.

If you need proof of the middle class’s power, look no further than this week’s midterms. Democrats just won both the House and the Senate.

The middle class is fed up with extremely wealthy politicians who provide tax breaks for their wealthy friends who don’t need them. Fed up with 28-year-old AI researchers buying $8 million homes in cash while their product threatens humanity’s existence. Fed up with high gas prices, high mortgage rates, a never-ending war, and a grocery bill that looks like a car payment.

When the largest class in America gets angry, it doesn’t write a strongly worded email. It votes. And it flips Congress.

The lesson is clear. If you want politicians to care about you, be middle class. If you want them to tax you, be rich. And if you want to be ignored and vilified at the same time, be upper middle class.

This is where we find ourselves with today’s post on how to be middle class and stay middle class, so you can feel safe, loved, and accepted.

Because based on the feedback from 3.5 million views and 1,400+ comments on X for my $408,000 budget post, I now clearly understand what is and isn’t allowed if you want to keep your middle class membership card.

So you think earning $408,000 a year is a lot of money? After working 50+ hours a week, being stressed out of your mind, and barely seeing your kids, it might not be the dream income you imagined.Look how quickly the money goes. $1/month left over.What would you cut? pic.twitter.com/AFGYHEdeQG

One of the biggest surprises from all the feedback on the $408,000 budget was that three weeks of vacation a year, two destination trips and one staycation, is considered an absurd luxury. The vast majority of commenters called it out of touch, ludicrous, or worse.

There was even a writer with 1.5 million Twitter followers who said he's never taken more than two weeks off in his life. He later explained it's because he doesn't make enough to take three weeks off. So if you needed more proof that writing doesn't pay, there it is.

Since 2009, I've considered three weeks of vacation a year the standard for anyone with a decade-plus of experience. This is a household making $408,000 with two kids. These aren't people fresh out of college. Taking 0-1 weeks in your first year or three, sure, that tracks. But after 10+ years in the workforce, three weeks off is not some wild ask.

Apparently it is. So the rule stands corrected: if you want to be middle class, cap your vacation at two weeks a year. Take a third week and you've officially graduated to out-of-touch elite. Congratulations, or condolences.

Not one of the 1,400 commenters gave this household credit for driving a paid-off Honda CR-V. A brand new, nicely equipped CR-V runs about $38,000, compared to roughly $50,000 for the average new car in America today.

So the verdict is in: the middle class are free, encouraged even, to spend more than they should on a car. This household is decidedly not following my 1/10th rule for car buying, the way personal finance nerds, the mass affluent, the rich, or the poor do.

Ironically, the nicer the car someone drives, up to a point, the more middle class they appear. It's understood that the middle class take on debt, or at least stretch, for a car nicer than their income comfortably supports. That's the badge. Wear it proudly, on 72-month financing.

There was a genuine uproar about this household paying $5,000/month for their mortgage. If you live in an expensive coastal city, $5,000 is reasonable, maybe even cheap, for a single-family home. But the feedback was clear: $5,000 is too much, and so is 2,100 square feet.

Three bedrooms and two bathrooms still reads as the quintessential middle class home to me, and I'll defend that. What's apparently not middle class is the 2,100 square feet of interior space. Plenty of commenters flagged that as immodest and far too big.

So if you want to keep your middle class card, buy or rent something under 2,000 square feet. Ideally something starting with a 1: 1,600, 1,800, even 1,990 sqft works. Just don't let that second digit round up to a 2.

With a modest home and an expensive car, this household also isn't following my House-To-Car Ratio guide either. The typical American runs a ratio of about 9 to 15. I recommend readers aim for at least 50, ideally 100+, so you're minimizing a depreciating liability while maximizing an appreciating asset. The middle class, evidently, prefer it the other way around.

Since a huge amount of education is now available for free online, paying for grade school is an unnecessary luxury the middle class shouldn't indulge in. Same logic applies to paying full freight for private or public university.

A smart, motivated kid with engaged parents will likely do fine in almost any academic setting. So dropping serious money on private grade school tuition is, by this logic, mostly a waste. Save and then invest it so the tuition money grows to over $1 million by the time they graduate college.

Then again, having over $1 million to give your 23-year-old college graduate because you saved and invested the difference disqualifies you from being middle class. So instead of saving and investing the difference, feel free to spend it on whatever your heart desires. YOLO!

Of course this household earns their $408,000 the hardest way possible: two W2 salaries, taxed at near the top of the marginal bracket before a single dollar hits their checking account. Federal, state, FICA, all of it withheld before they ever see it.

Nobody in the comments seemed bothered by this part. And that's the tell. The middle class don't just tolerate a W2 job, they build an identity around it. Steady paycheck, steady withholding, steady sense that this is simply how adults earn money.

Ask them about starting something of their own, a side business, a rental, an app, anything with equity attached, and the answer is almost always the same: too risky. Better to let somebody else take the risk, and the upside, while you collect a salary and help build their dream instead of your own.

Entrepreneurship isn't guaranteed. But neither is a W2 job in a layoff cycle. The middle class would rather have the certainty of somebody else's risk than the upside of their own.

If you're a working middle class professional, the going rate is two kids, maybe. Every additional kid means more daycare, more food, more square footage you're apparently not supposed to have, and no extra help from the government to offset any of it.

But stop working, or work little enough to qualify for subsidies, and suddenly more children becomes affordable again. Medicaid, subsidized childcare, food assistance, the EITC, all of it scales with need, not with effort.

The system isn't rewarding family size. It's rewarding low reported income, and if you're grinding away at a W2 job trying to move up, you're the one least likely to qualify for help raising the family you're working so hard to support.

So the rule holds. Two working parents, two kids, tops. Three kids or more puts you in the rich category. The middle class don’t have what some call trophy kids given it can take a fortune to raise them.

Curiously, there was a lot of pushback about the $408,000 household saving $16,000 a year for their two children's college tuition. Commenters asked why they'd bother saving for college when they're already paying $45,000 a year for private grade school per kid.

My initial response was that college can be far more expensive than $45,000 a year. Some universities now cost $100,000 to $120,000 a year, all-in. So setting aside additional money for college isn't a bad idea. But critics said the parents should just pay for college out of their day job income when the time comes.

Therefore, the middle class do not proactively save and invest for college in tax-advantaged 529 plans. And given many top private universities now charge $0 tuition for households earning under $200,000 a year, the belief that saving for college is unnecessary is, in fairness, somewhat logical.

What's illogical is assuming your kid has a real shot at one of these schools with a sub-7% acceptance rate. But the middle class are hopeful their children will defy the odds when such schools cater predominantly to the rich.

As an older parent, it never occurred to me not to save for college, because I don't plan on working to pay for it when the time comes. When my youngest goes to college, I'll be 61. There's no way I want to be grinding from 61 to 64, paying tuition, as someone who left the workforce in 2012. So the only way for me to pay for college is to save and build passive income.

Therefore, it's clear the middle class don't focus on building passive income through a taxable brokerage account, a rental property portfolio, or other taxable investments. The middle class are solely focused on contributing to tax-advantaged retirement accounts like the 401(k), IRA, and Roth IRA, which is great, until you actually want to stop working before 59 and a half.

Having the grit to work forever, whether you want to or not, is truly a wonderful trait of the middle class. It makes employers proud.

If any of this sounds absurd, that's the point. We judge other people's money and choices against our own experience, then call the mismatch a moral failing.

Personally, I love being part of the middle class, because nobody bothers you if you're like most people. But this exercise made me realize I've been too frugal in FIRE. I also need to build more grit by mentally preparing to work longer while spending more as a reward. So I'll do those things to retain my middle class membership card.

The $408,000 family isn't doing anything wrong. They're just the latest Rorschach test for what the internet has decided “middle class” is allowed to look like. We shouldn’t ignore the opinions of the 3.5 million viewers and 1,400+ commenters.

And if working until 70 because you never built passive income is the price of admission, then welcome to the club. Just make sure to invest some money in our overlords to avoid being part of the permanent underclass. Now that would be unacceptable.

Readers, do you agree with my conditions and descriptions of what makes someone middle class? What are some other conditions you might add?

If you want an actual framework for these decisions, car versus house, rent versus buy, private school versus public, W2 versus building your own thing, instead of a crowdsourced vibe check, that's what I wrote Buy This Not That for. It walks through the exact tradeoffs above (the 1/10th car rule, the House-To-Car Ratio) without the 1,400 comments telling you you're doing it wrong either way.

At the end of the day, everyone has a different definition of what middle class and financial independence means. What seems extravagant to one person may be totally normal to someone else. And what one person deems as a sound financial decision could be completely irrational to another.

I do hope more people learn how to live within their means, save, invest, keep debt in check and responsibly enjoy what they’ve worked hard for.

Keisha Lance Bottoms Makes History as First Black Woman Elected Governor

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Keisha Lance Bottoms has won the Georgia governor’s race, becoming the first Black female governor elected in the United States. Bottoms defeated Republican billionaire businessman Rick Jackson, marking the first time the swing state has elected a Democratic governor in more than two decades.

The victory was a highlight in a massively successful night for Democrats in one of the most bitter midterm elections in recent history, one that saw the Democratic Party win total control of Congress in a nationwide rebuke of Donald Trump and the Republican Party.

Democratic Senate candidates won big in battleground states including Georgia, where Jon Ossoff was re-elected; Texas, where Democrat James Talarico defeated Republican state Attorney General Ken Paxton; and South Carolina, where pediatrician Annie Andrews claimed the South Carolina Senate seat. In another historic first, Democrat Chris Pappas won New Hampshire’s U.S. Senate race, and will be the first openly gay male senator with his victory over Republican John Sununu.

Bottoms, a former Atlanta mayor who also served as an adviser in the Biden administration, won despite a colossal spending disadvantage against Jackson, who pumped tens of millions of his own fortune into his campaign.

Bottoms, like other Democrats across the nation, wielded frustration over the Trump administration to bolster a campaign that remained tightly focused on taking on the rising cost of living and quality of life issues. During her bid for governor, Bottoms proposed allowing local municipalities to eliminate the sales tax on groceries along with taxes on diapers, baby formula and feminine hygiene products. She also proposed doing away with the state income tax for public school teachers and the state’s tax on car insurance premiums, homeowner’s insurance, and gas. Bottoms also vowed to expand Medicaid and to pause the construction of new data centers in Georgia. Trending Stories Trump Is Already Crying That He’s Not to Blame for Republican Wipeout Elizabeth Holmes' Partner Did an AMA About That Shower Scene, His Sniffing, and 'You Can See Everything' Democrats Win Total Control of Congress Mick Jagger Praises Sam Fender’s Rolling Stones Cover

“Good evening, Georgia,” Bottoms began in her victory speech Tuesday night. “Over the last year and a half, we have traveled from the red hills of Georgia to the Blue Ridge Mountains of the Piedmont, from the coastal shores to rolling farmlands, from urban streets to rural communities, and with each stop, your faces, your families, your communities, your hopes and your dreams, and even your disappointments have been imprinted upon my heart, this campaign was about every community in every corner of Georgia,” she continued. “Georgia, this win is for all of us. Thank you, Georgia. I am honored to be your 84th governor.”

French bank Crédit Mutuel Arkéa exploited for over $133 million, ZachXBT says

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

ZachXBT said he has a 'high degree of confidence' that the attacker is not associated with North Korea's Lazarus Group.

Onchain investigator ZachXBT has identified French cooperative bank Crédit Mutuel Arkéa as the alleged victim of a theft estimated at more than $133 million.

ZachXBT first reported on Nov. 2 that an unnamed EU entity had lost over $106 million from multiple centralized exchange accounts the previous day, with funds withdrawn as USDC and USDT.

A day later, he raised the total stolen amount to above $133 million after tracing an additional $27 million in withdrawals, some of which occurred after his initial disclosure. He speculated that a large financial institution in Western Europe was exploited and currently being covered up.

On the evening of Nov. 3, he stated that the institution is allegedly Crédit Mutuel Arkéa, a French bank with approximately $235 billion in assets. He noted that the attack spanned multiple days and suggested the actual losses could be higher when accounting for possible over-the-counter activity.

"Over the past 24 hours the illicit actor has been aggressively swapping USDC for ETH, transferring to instant exchanges (WellWallet, Kyrrex, etc), converting to XMR via Wagyu, and bridging across multiple chains," ZachXBT wrote in his Nov. 2 message in his Telegram group, adding that he has a "high degree of confidence" that the attacker is not associated with North Korea's Lazarus Group.

He has criticized the institution for allegedly allowing more than $30 million to be laundered, noting that proper incident disclosure to EU law enforcement could have frozen the funds.

Crédit Mutuel Arkéa has not issued any public statement confirming or denying the incident. The Block has reached out to the bank but has not heard back. The claims remain based on ZachXBT's own analysis and have not been independently verified by the bank or authorities.

The bank has more than 5.6 million customers, with total assets of 211.5 billion euros (around $237 billion).

The Legend Of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time On Switch 2 Has Been Updated

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Some parts of the world are now just hours away from the launch of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake for Switch 2, and ahead of this, Nintendo has rolled out a new game update.

The anticipated release has been bumped from Version 1.0.0 to Version 1.1.1. Next time you check your game, you should be prompted to update. We had a look and can confirm our copy is also running on Version 1.1.1.

Based on the "manage software" section in the Switch 2 option menu, the remake is now 24.8GB in size, and this latest update is around 343MB to install. There are no patch notes just yet, but when we get an update about what's included, we'll let you know.

"Day one" and pre-release updates like this are pretty standard for Nintendo games nowadays, with the company often rolling them out to activate certain online features such as multiplayer. Star Fox also got a day one update when it returned earlier this year. Apart from overhauled graphics and gameplay updates, this latest remake includes support for amiibo and the Switch app Zelda Notes.

Again, if we hear any updates about this latest patch, we'll let you know. Have you been prompted to update your copy of Ocarina of Time yet? Have you got it digitally downloaded and ready to go, or are you picking up a physical copy? Let us know in the comments.

Karen Bass and Nithya Raman in Tight Race for L.A. Mayor in Early Returns

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

'I'm very hopeful,' Raman says of her chances; Bass leads election night crowd in chanting 'Four more years'

Karen Bass and Nithya Raman are in a tight race for L.A. mayor in early returns, and the race may not be called for days.

At Raman’s election-night event in the plaza of the Greek Theatre, the mood was buoyant as the crowd watched Democrat wins steadily march across the South and East.

“I can’t believe South Carolina,” a young male Raman supporter enthused to his crowd, which milled around the open-air space outside the historic venue tucked into the Los Feliz foothills. Related Stories ‘Father’s House,’ Starring David Dencik and Trine-Dyrholm, Picked Up by DR Sales Ahead of Tallinn Competition

Raman addressed her supporters shortly after 11 p.m. She conceded no ground to Bass. “I’m very hopeful,” she told the crowd as the vote count showed a razor-thin margin of difference between them.

Raman, who first joined the Los Angeles City Council in 2020, came out swinging against the L.A. political establishment. “This city’s government has made solving problems look impossible for so long,” Raman said. “Hopelessness can be fatal for a city.”

Raman also sought to cast herself as part of Los Angeles’ long history of serving as a haven for cutting-edge organizations and individuals of uncommon ambition. The legacy that stretches from the movie business to UCLA’s role in sending “the very first message across the internet” has made the city “the cultural capital of the entire planet,” Raman said.

In an unguarded moment, Raman seemed to nod to criticism of her political agenda and leadership style that she’s received since launching her surprise campaign for mayor in February. “Radical and divisive, right here,” she said with a grin.

Bass addressed her supporters nearby at the Loews Hollywood Hotel around 9:30 p.m., leading with the news that the Democrats had taken both the House and the Senate. In her own race, she acknowledged the results might not be known for some time.

“It takes a while to count the vote in our city,” she said, but added she was confident that in the end, “we will achieve victory.”

She then led the crowd in a chant of “Four more years!”

At Raman’s event, giant-screen TVs lined the plaza, offering election updates, though virtually everyone was following the polls on phone apps. The young crowd roared at the news of Democrat Gina Hinojosa’s historic upset victory in the Texas gubernatorial race.

Early returns from Los Angeles showing Raman in a near dead heat with Bass were seen as a positive sign by Raman’s faithful supporters. The crowd was packed with man buns, UAW T-shirts, UCLA sweatshirts and Dodger caps. The conversation focused on the future of Democratic Socialists of America and what the blue wave means for this rising progressive movement that Raman has been connected to in the past.

Raman campaign manager Ian Manheimer tried to pep up the crowd around 8:45 p.m., but there was still no sign of the candidate herself. No matter what happens after the votes are counted, he assured the crowd that their full-throated support for Raman’s abbreviated run for mayor has made a difference. “You have pushed the city forward,” he said.

The Bass team’s attitude before the 8 p.m. ballot deadline was one of confidence and regularity. The mayoral incumbent had been here before and her staffers were acting like it, calmly arranging the Lowes Hollywood Ballroom into their election HQ and leaving room for familiar small talk, hugs and light joking.

Outside of Bass’ early encouraging poll numbers, the historic Democratic midterm victories rolling through in the background were an obvious mood booster Tuesday night. As Mark Ramos, chair of the Los Angeles Democratic Party, entered the hall he yelled to an expectant line of camera people, “It’s a good day to be a Democrat!”

The Kalshi odds, which had been slightly favoring Bass before the first ballots were counted, tipped heavily in Raman’s favor afterward. But Bass’ allies sought to convey an upbeat note. “The mayor — her closing has been really, really strong,” Ramos said. “I think we’re going to get a good return when those ballots begin to get counted. I think the mayor got a real wind at her back the last couple weeks.”

When asked about L.A.’s biggest problems, the words on everyone’s lips were affordability, homelessness and housing. Bass has faced criticism for not tackling all three issues more aggressively during her first term, but her fans seemed confident she would finish the job if allowed four more years. “Karen Bass deserves four more years, and that’s why organized labor is kicking ass for Karen Bass and we will continue to kick ass for Karen Bass until she is reelected,” said Yvonne Wheeler, the chair of the Los Angeles County Federation of Labor.

Voters repeatedly cited trust and familiarity as reasons for voting for Bass. Abby Sher, a 71-year-old lifelong Angelino, has followed Bass’ political career since the ‘70s and praised her early work with the Los Angeles community support group Liberty Hill Foundation. She sees her challenger, Nithya Raman, as an inexperienced outsider, citing the fact that she’s only lived in Los Angeles for 13 years.

“I’m hoping that other people are aware of the substantive difference between the two of them,” Sher said.

For Michael Guy, a 57-year-old consultant who has lived in L.A. for the past 33 years, Bass isn’t his ideal candidate. But with the 2027 Super Bowl and 2028 Olympics, a change in leadership wouldn’t be a “good thing for the city.”

“Sometimes you got to take the dog with the fleas,” he said. “I don’t have anything with Nithya, but the reality is that Karen has more of a track history, good or bad.”

Bass has faced a considerable challenge in winning back voters who are upset about homelessness, civic disorder and her handling of the 2025 Palisades fire. She has sought to acknowledge mistakes while warning that Raman, a lefty councilwoman, would be worse.

Raman, for her part, has cast herself as an outsider going up against a City Hall establishment that is mired in self-serving mediocrity. She has attacked Bass’ record in office and argued that L.A. should set a higher standard.

Raman has been critical of Bass’ handling of the exodus of film and TV jobs, saying that Bass was late to appoint a liaison to the industry and noting the appointee is not full-time. She has pledged to staff a full film office to serve the industry.

Both candidates have also said they will champion unlimited film and TV tax credits at the state level, and push for a federal tax incentive to put the U.S. on equal footing with countries abroad.

Raman had the backing of the Writers Guild of America West, of which her husband is a member; Bass was endorsed by the Hollywood Teamsters, IATSE and the American Federation of Musicians Local 47.

Cynthia Littleton contributed to this report.

Atalanta BC vs. Kairat - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

By Seye Omidiora | 04 Nov 2026 04:48, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 05:02

Atalanta BC need to respond to Monday's humiliating 3-0 Serie A defeat to Parma when they host Kairat in the Conference League on Thursday.

The defeat prompted Maurizio Sarri to criticise his players' mentality as they entered a "ritiro" ahead of facing a Kairat side now managed by Zhora Arutyunyan following Rafael Urazbakhtin's sudden resignation.

Atalanta had gone four matches without winning before Parma visited Bergamo on Monday night, but the ensuing defeat was still jarring.

The Emilians were winless in four matches, losing three and failing to score in three while languishing in the bottom three; however, Atalanta's collapse in front of their own supporters angered Sarri enough to make a training retreat his response.

Players are no longer permitted to return home ahead of Thursday's European visit from Kairat, as La Dea seek to arrest a worrying slump that has brought three defeats in five matches and six goals conceded across the other two fixtures, draws of 4-4 and 2-2 with Frosinone and Fiorentina respectively.

Sarri's men have conceded 14 goals across their five-match winless run, letting in at least two in every fixture to underline their frailties ahead of Conference League matchday three.

The Bergamaschi sit 15th after two rounds, winning once before losing 2-0 to Ajax last time out, but their position in Europe's third-tier club competition is far from irretrievable.

Kairat, though, are in a worse position than the Nerazzurri, with the Kazakhstani outfit winless after two matches following a draw with Riga on matchday one and a heavy 5-1 home defeat to Panathinaikos in the previous round.

That Conference League defeat on October 22 formed part of a three-match winless run and a sequence of one victory in four fixtures, leading to Urazbakhtin's resignation and Arutyunyan's subsequent appointment.

The two-time title-winning manager's departure was unexpected, but the Yellow Blacks cannot dwell on it for long, with their continental prospects at risk of further damage if they suffer consecutive defeats in the competition this week.

Despite Atalanta's own struggles, Kairat's prospects appear bleak given their failure to win any of their last four European away fixtures across Champions League and Europa League qualifying and Conference League action.

Sary-Qaralar suffered 1-0 defeats to both Omonia and Levski Sofia and lost 3-0 to Anderlecht before ending their scoreless run and sequence of defeats with a 1-1 draw at Riga last month.

Those struggles may therefore give Atalanta hope of a response, although the Yellow Blacks will seek an improved performance to compound the Bergamaschi's misery during their extended winless run.

Franck Kessie (calf), Sead Kolasinac (thigh), Giacomo Raspadori (thigh) and Gianluca Scamacca (thigh) are believed to be touch-and-go for Atalanta, while Thomas Kristensen (muscle), Raoul Bellanova (thigh), Isak Hien (muscle), Odilon Kossounou (thigh) and Gianluca Gaetano (muscle) are sidelined as the hosts contend with a spate of muscular injuries.

Nikola Krstovic remains Atalanta's leading goal threat with six Serie A strikes, and the forward could make his first European appearance of the season if Scamacca is not ready to start.

Kairat have fewer injury concerns, with Jorginho the visitors' only absentee, having not featured since July.

While Kairat have had two different goalscorers in the Conference League, Marc Gual and Edmilson have both reached double figures domestically, combining for 22 league goals to establish themselves as Sary-Qaralar's leading attacking threats.

With Krstovic potentially leading the attack, La Dea should have enough quality to end their five-match winless run and ease the pressure on Sarri.

India’s EloElo nabs $80M from NBCUniversal to bet on the country’s rising microdrama market

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

More than 118 million users watch microdramas every month as the serialized format has taken off in India. EloElo’s Story TV is one of the top apps for microdramas, with over 33 million active users, according to analysts.

Banking on that scale, the company has raised $80 million in Series C from Hornbill Capital and NBCUniversal. This round brings its total fundraising to $130 million, with a valuation of $500 million.

As people spend more time on platforms hosting verticalized videos, serialized content has become popular in multiple geographies, including the U.S., China, and India, with Hollywood celebrities like Issa Rae, James Franco, and Kim Kardashian investing in and starring in the format.

Given India’s massive internet population of more than a billion and the country’s appetite for entertainment content, it presents a big market opportunity, with analysts expecting the industry to cross $3-$4 billion in annual revenue by 2030. Last year, dedicated apps like Story TV, Kuku TV, and Quick TV were among the top downloaded video streaming apps in the country.

Founded in 2020 by former Flipkart exec Saurabh Pandey, the Story TV platform has taken off in the last few years with a catalog of over 5,000 titles in six languages. Pandey told TechCrunch that because of the snackable content format, users log 95 minutes on the app on average through multiple sessions in a day.

“I fundamentally believe that the next billion users will probably not grow up on a diet of watching television; they will grow up on a diet of watching content on their phones, right? And this content has to be, and this entertainment, designed very differently. It’s not going to be appointment viewing. It’s going to be within certain slots throughout the day. And a lot of this was earlier solved for short clips, but serialized storytelling was never solved,” Pandey said about the microdrama format.

He believes that there still will be a place for big-screen viewing with family or friends, but the majority of entertainment consumption would be done through mobile.

All microdrama platforms compete against platforms like YouTube, Instagram, and TikTok, where creators are posting content for free. Some of them are also creating a way for creators to post serialized content. Pandey thinks that producing quality content on the platform will entice users to choose StoryTV over other free platforms. The company points to its partnerships, such as the one with Indian TV production company Balaji Telefilms, to produce content.

The company offers different kinds of subscriptions ranging from monthly, quarterly, and yearly to unlock content. It also offers some free content supported by ads, which might not be as lucrative a medium to make money as subscriptions, according to reports.

Monetization is a challenge in India, as users often stick around for free content, but few convert to paid users. A report from analyst firm Redseer indicates that more than 40 million people have paid for microdrama at least once, but only 15-17 million users pay for it on a monthly basis. A separate survey by Ormax Media suggested that 72% of people haven’t ever paid while consuming microdrama.

All microdrama platforms want to have enough supply to engage different audiences and want to involve AI to solve that. Some platforms, like Google-backed Kuku, want to improve workflows and create more content, and some platforms like Dashverse’s Shortfree want to use video AI models to create serialized clips.

At the moment, StoryTV falls into the first camp with its Dolphin AI platform. Started as an internal tool to improve video creation, the company is now planning to make the tool widely available. It can act as an editor with templates and an array of models to create images, audio, and video, according to its website.

EloElo’s vision is to have people use this tool for marketing campaigns and ads as well. Notably, while Indian platforms like Pocket and Kuku have built AI tools and given them to creators, it is likely to accelerate their content pipeline. But at InVideo, there hasn’t been a scaled effort to provide a good alternative to CapCut or Instagram Edits to creators from India.

Pandey believes that human involvement is still paramount to producing good stories, and AI tools are still creative directors for steering.

“My belief in involving AI is that AI is a really fast race car where AI is the engine, but you need an extremely capable Formula One driver to be on the steering wheel. That, for me, is the creative director or the showrunner. AI is supposed to work with them, augmenting their skill set. AI can help write stories, but so far the best stories I have loved have all had a very high human x AI element to it,” he said.

Besides microdrama, the company also has a vertical video learning app, Master, competing with the likes of Seekho and Kuku’s Guru, and an AI companion app, Voxy AI. The company said that it has more than 40 million monthly users across its apps. While it didn’t bifurcate users by app, the majority of users belong to Story TV.

EloElo currently has 240 people and wants to hire to increase the size of its AI and machine learning research team. The company is also deploying a good chunk of its capital to produce more original titles.

MIT Investment Co., along with existing investors like Play Ventures, Kalaari Capital, Waterbridge Ventures, and Griffin Gaming Partners, invested in the round.

Hasan Piker Threatens Further Legal Action Against Conservative Pundits He Claims Have Defamed Him

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Twitch streamer's legal team recently filed a $72.5 million defamation lawsuit against CNN contributor Scott Jennings.

After filing a $72.5 million defamation lawsuit against CNN contributor Scott Jennings last week, the progressive political Twitch streamer Hasan Piker‘s legal team is promising more legal action against conservative pundits they feel have defamed their client.

In an interview with Vanity Fair, Piker’s attorney and defamation specialist Daniel Watkins said there is a “reckoning coming.” “There are other people and other entities that will likely receive outreach from my law firm in the coming days,” Watkins said.

The more aggressive legal posture from Piker’s camp stands in marked contrast to his previous tendency to let the more controversial, and often baseless, accusations against him slide. Since he became a national and international political figure — he has been namechecked by both Donald Trump and Benjamin Netanyahu in 2026 — Piker has faced a torrent of claims against him, including animal abuse, racism and misogyny, and has become something of a lightning rod for Republican and centrist Democratic attacks. Related Stories Business Hasan Piker Files $72.5 Million Defamation Lawsuit Against Scott Jennings Over Lynching Quote General News Wild! Mattel Sets UNO Card-Game Competition Series to Stream on Twitch

That all changed on Aug. 11, when Jennings, appearing on his Salem Media podcast, presented a spliced video montage alleging that Piker advocated for lynchings. Jennings used the completely out-of-context segment to urge Michigan voters to reject Senatorial candidate Abdul El-Sayed over his ties to the streamer.

Watkins issued a formal retraction demand to Salem Media’s chief legal counsel, Christopher Henderson, in late August. When that yielded no response, Piker filed suit, demanding $27.5 million in compensatory damages and $45 million in punitive damages.

“I have been very deliberately anti-litigation for a very long time,” Piker told Vanity Fair. “But something shifted that’s been bubbling up where… people like Scott Jennings, and there are others as well, started trafficking in outright lies, defamation, and slander.”

Watkins stressed the severe impact of the clips on Piker’s career, calling the targeted misattributions “disgusting and vile and dangerous” for a public figure built on advocacy for marginalized groups. For Piker, the legal action stems from personal fallout — including reputational damage and safety concerns after an individual stalked him at a public event.

“When is the last time you experienced eight consistent months of [mainstream media] having one primary villain?” Piker asked. “It’s not fun at all. It sucks.”

With midterms wrapping up, with big wins for Piker-backed candidates, the streamer and his legal team are already eyeing their next potential targets. Piker highlighted former RNC chair Ronna McDaniel, who repeated the lynching claim on C-SPAN, and Sen. Ted Cruz, who reshared Jennings’ montage on X. Piker publicly taunted the Texas senator online shortly after filing his suit against Jennings, posting: “Hey Ted you seen this?”

Speaking directly to people who have made false claims against him, Piker added, “If you felt as though you could outright fabricate quotes and make up totally ridiculous claims about me, you should probably work on your retractions immediately.”

Democrats Win Total Control of Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Democratic Party won total control of Congress in the midterm elections, part of a seismic, nationwide rebuke of Donald Trump and the Republican Party.

The party was widely expected to win back control of the House of Representatives, but less certain was the balance of the Senate. Democrats won them both, though, as part of a hugely successful night capping off one of the most chaotic, contested, and bitter midterm elections in recent history.

Riding a wave of voter turnout amid an economic crisis started by Republican President Donald Trump, Democratic Senate candidates won big in the battleground states including Texas, Georgia, and South Carolina.

With a majority in the Senate and House, Democrats are now free to pursue an ambitious legislative agenda during Trump’s last two years in office. Trump is entering his lame duck period as a deeply unpopular president beset by scandal and crises of his own making. While he will likely use his presidential veto to keep many of a Democratic Congress’ bills from passing, control over both Houses will give the Democrats a powerful platform to both investigate Trump’s actions in the first half of his second term and set the tone for their agenda going into the next open presidential election in 2028.

“They will turn every committee of Congress into an investigative body, and they’ll go after the president’s family, the Cabinet, his donors and friends,” Speaker of the House Mike Johnson told a room of Christian conservatives earlier this year. “Half of you in this room will be targeted. I run the protection program. I’ll take care of you,” he added.

In the House, the powerful House Oversight Committee is ready to go to work. Their primary target may be Donald Trump’s family businesses, which have dramatically increased in value since the beginning of his second term.

“Every day, Trump gives Congress something new to investigate,” Democratic Rep. Greg Caesar told Rolling Stone last month. “Democrats need to be ready to dig into any and all of it on day one.”

With the Senate under their control, the House’s investigative power gets more support in the upper house of Congress. While Democrats will likely still be far short of the two-thirds supermajority needed to convict Trump in any future impeachment trial, they’ll have enough sway to make sure that any charges the House brings won’t be laughed off by a sycophantic GOP.  Trending Stories Trump Is Already Crying That He’s Not to Blame for Republican Wipeout Elizabeth Holmes' Partner Did an AMA About That Shower Scene, His Sniffing, and 'You Can See Everything' Democrats Win Total Control of Congress Mick Jagger Praises Sam Fender’s Rolling Stones Cover

The balance of the Supreme Court, too, could change as a result of the blue wave. Justice Samuel Alito is nearing retirement, and had the Republicans retained control of the Senate, it was possible Alito would have let Trump fill his fourth vacancy on the court before leaving office in 2029.

All of these battles, however, are still to come. But for tonight, the Democrats must feel as though they’re a little bit closer to winning American politics’ endless war.

Democrats Win House and Senate in Sweeping Rout of GOP

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

In an outcome seen as a backlash to President Donald Trump, Democrats decisively won control of the U.S. Senate and the House of Representatives in the 2026 midterm elections.

The Senate’s flip to the Democrats was called by CNN at 11:35 p.m. ET, with the network projecting the party had won at least 51 seats to lock up the voting majority, after cable network NewsNation had predicted the Senate would go blue about 15 minutes prior. The Dems’ win of the House was called by MS NOW just before 10 p.m. ET followed by CNN several minutes later, then NBC News and CBS News. The AP had not called either the Senate or House for the Democrats as of this writing. Related Stories ‘Father’s House,’ Starring David Dencik and Trine-Dyrholm, Picked Up by DR Sales Ahead of Tallinn Competition

In the widely followed the Senate race in Texas, former state representative James Talarico, a Democrat, was the projected winner over Ken Paxton, the state’s Republican attorney general. It was the most expensive U.S. Senate campaign to date, with an estimated $573.2 million spent on political ads, including at $168 million from Elon Musk, who backed Paxton, per CNN.

“We just took back Texas,” Talarico said in his victory speech. “We just proved that all the money in the world is no match for the people of Texas. … And we just sent a message to every billionaire megadonor, every trillionaire megadonor, every corrupt defender of this broken political system: In this state, in this country, power belongs to the people.”

In other key Senate races, Roy Cooper decisively beat Michael Whatley in North Carolina; Annie Andrews prevailed in South Carolina over Darline Graham, sister of the late Lindsey Graham; Adam Hamilton won in Kansas to become the state’s first Democratic senator in almost 90 years; Sherrod Brown won against Republican incumbent Jon Husted in Ohio; and Josh Turek, a Democratic state representative, flipped Iowa’s open Senate seat in a victory over Rep. Ashley Hinson. In Maine, Troy Jackson defeated GOP incumbent Sen. Susan Collins.

One of the Senate races that remained too close to call was the fierce battle in Michigan between Dr. Abdul El-Sayed and Republican Mike Rogers.

“This is the repudiation of Trumpism that people have been hoping for,” CNN political commentator and former Obama adviser Van Jones said about the “Blue Wave” sweeping across the country. MS NOW’s Rachel Maddow said of the Democrats’ House win: “This is a seismic change in Washington.”

With the Democrats retaking the House, Rep. Hakeem Jeffries of New York’s 8th District, who is currently House minority leader and won reelection Tuesday, is in line to become Speaker of the House.

The surge of support for Democrats comes as Trump’s approval ratings among American voters has plummeted over the course of the year, amid higher gas and grocery prices and the administration’s unpopular war with Iran. As of Nov. 3, just 37% of U.S. voters approve of Trump, near his lowest level ever of 36% just two weeks ago, per the New York Times’ analysis of multiple polls. Trump has stumped for Republican candidates at rallies across the country in recent weeks.

Trump, after repeatedly urging voters in battleground states to vote as if he were on the ballot, wrote in a post on his Truth Social platform Tuesday evening, “I wasn’t on the Ballot and if I was, we would have won IN A LANDSLIDE!” He also blamed Fox News for the Republican defeats, saying the network “bears responsibility for these losses by constantly and consistently putting on the Dumocrat opposition.” Trump told the New York Times he was “absolutely willing” to accept the election results and that “We’ll, you know, look forward to working with” the Democrats: “We’ll probably work something out.”

In gubernatorial races, notable Democratic winners Tuesday night included Keisha Lance Bottoms, who prevailed in the Georgia gubernatorial race, becoming the first Black woman governor in the U.S. Amy Acton defeated former Republican presidential candidate and Trump ally Vivek Ramaswamy in the Ohio governor’s race, and Gina Hinojosa beat incumbent Texas Gov. Greg Abbott.

Pictured above: James Talarico, senator-elect of Texas

Choosin' Texas I Can Tell

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Mostly law professors | Sometimes contrarian | Often libertarian | Always independent

A few moments ago, the networks called the Texas Senate and Governor races for the Democratic candidates. Those outcomes will get a lot of attention. But of significant interest will be the downballot judicial races in Texas.

This year, four of the nine seats on the Texas Supreme Court were on the ballot. Unless something unexpected happens, all four will lose: Chief Justice Jimmy Blacklock, Justice James P. Sullivan, Justice Kyle Hawkins, and Justice Brett Busby. These are all brilliant judges. Three of them clerked on the United States Supreme Court. Chief Justice Blacklock has been a strong leader, following in the footsteps of Chief Justice Nathan Hecht. But the qualifications do not matter. Everything was on the party line. The Texas Supreme Court will now have five Republican members and four Democratic members. One might expect a number of 5-4 cases for controversial cases. Then again, the Chief Justice is now the administrator of the state court system, which entails some important responsibilities.

The Texas Court of Criminal Appeals (the court of last resort for criminal cases) had three seats on the ballot. Again, unless something happens, all three will lose. There will still be a six-member conservative majority. I don't see too much changing on that Court.

Recently, the Fifteenth Court of Appeals was created as a statewide constitutional court. Virtually all suits against the government are routed to that court. (Think of something like the D.C. Circuit.) All three members of that court were on the ballot. Unless something happens, all three will lose. The Court will now have 100% Democratic members. I would expect a lot mandamus suits to the Texas Supreme Court.

I haven't had time to go through the returns from the local courts, but it appears that most of the incumbent republican judges will lose office. This will be an even bigger bloodbath than 2018, when the Beto wave wiped out nearly all of the Republican judges in Houston, Austin, Dallas, and other large cities. So much judicial experience for non-controversial judges will go down the tube.

The Governor of Texas is fairly weak, as compared to other states, but he does have the power to appoint new members of the Texas Supreme Court and other courts when vacancies arise. Recently, the Texas legislature created a new Texas Business Court. This court was designed to compete with Delaware. Many of the current Texas business courts are not likely to be reappointed. This election will significantly affect businesses that chose to make Texas their headquarters. Speaking of which…

The Texas Attorney General Race will also likely go to the Democratic candidate. I would suspect that all of the conservative strategic litigation cases brought by Attorney General Paxton will be dismissed. Indeed, it is conceivable that the new Texas Attorney General would not defend the Texas Ten Commandments Law if cert is granted. (The BIO will be filed soon.) The Texas Attorney General will also not bring original jurisdiction actions before the Supreme Court of Texas. Going forward, all of the strategic forums in Texas will be severely underutilized. The only court where the Texas Attorney General might file cases will be in Austin. I should note that one seat on the Western District of Texas remained vacant for two years, and will likely remain vacant. But beyond the strategic litigation, the Texas Attorney General will now be able to investigate all of the corporations that moved to Texas. There are a lot of them.

What about judicial nominees? Despite my best efforts, Judge Southwick will get the last laugh. Todd Butler will almost certainly be confirmed. But the Fifth Circuit will likely be a lot quieter, given the lack of Texas Attorney General cases. I also hope this means that Judge Rudofsky will get a vote, as that Eighth Circuit seat would remain vacant for the foreseeable future. Indeed, the Eighth Circuit may be the only viable forum for a Republican AG to bring cases starting in 2026.

Going forward, Senators Cruz and Talarico will have to agree on blue slips for District Court nominees. Still, I suspect zero nominees will get a vote over the next two years unless there is some deal struck with the Senate Democrats.

What about Justices Alito and Thomas? They did what Justice Breyer could not--resist the pressure. I hope they live long and prosper.

Ultimately, I'm reminded of something I tell my students--we are never more than two years away from a midterm election. Despite threats about fascism and totalitarianism, the people are able to effect change.

Former Sen. Sherrod Brown defeats Jon Husted, dealing a blow to crypto PAC Fairshake

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Earlier this year, Fairshake threw millions of dollars into the Ohio race, positioning itself against former Sen. Sherrod Brown.

Former Democratic Sen. Sherrod Brown is taking back his spot in the Senate, leaving crypto political action committee Fairshake with the bag as he beats out incumbent Republican Jon Husted.

On Tuesday night, Brown defeated Husted in the race for Ohio's Senate seat, dealing a setback to Fairshake, a top crypto political action committee that committed tens of millions of dollars to keep Brown out of Washington.

Earlier this year, Fairshake threw $30 million into the Ohio race, positioning itself against Brown. The crypto PAC and the broader crypto industry played a significant role in the 2024 elections, spending millions to influence political outcomes.

This is not Fairshake's first time spending against Brown. He was previously the chair of the Senate Banking Committee, where he set the agenda for crypto and called for a crackdown on the use of cryptocurrency to fund terrorism and evade sanctions.

Then in 2024, Fairshake spent $12 million to back crypto-friendly Republican Bernie Moreno against Brown, defeating him. Since Democrats regained control of the Senate, Brown could become chair of the Senate Agriculture Committee, which has jurisdiction over digital assets and prediction markets, among other things. If he is chair, sources previously told The Block that Brown would be unlikely to introduce any crypto legislation.

Sen. Husted, who is endorsed by crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto, has voted yes for a few crypto bills, including the broad crypto legislation called the Clarity Act. Husted has an A rating from the group and gave Brown an F.

Other candidates backed by Stand With Crypto such as Democrat Chris Pappas in New Hampshire won their race.

Other pivotal Senate wins include Democratic Sens. Cory Booker and Mark Warner, both of whom have been a part of the negotiations for crypto legislation. Senate Democratic candidate in Texas, James Talarico, received an A rating from crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto for his previous pro-crypto statements and his vote to create a strategic bitcoin reserve in Texas. Talarico also won his race on Tuesday against Texas Attorney General Ken Paxton.

MAGA wiped out in blue avalanche

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The signs were there from the outset. About 30 minutes after the polls in Kentucky’s 6th Congressional District closed at 6 p.m., the first numbers from rural counties began trickling in. It was no surprise that they favored Republican Ralph Alvarado over Democrat Zach Dembo. The district was reliably Democratic until 2012, when Andy Barr, a Republican who is now running to replace Mitch McConnell in the Senate, eked out a win. Since then, the GOP has gerrymandered the district to all but ensure it stayed in the fold.

The real story, though, could be found in the margins. Alvarado was underperforming Barr’s 2024 results by around 20 percentage points — a staggering drop, indicating that a significant number of Republicans had either stayed home or decided to take a chance on Dembo. Off the Democratic map for the last 14 years, by 9:15 p.m., when it was reported that Alvarado had conceded, the district had become a microcosm of what was unfolding nationwide.

The blue landslide, long predicted by pundits but mentioned with fingers crossed by Democrats, was becoming an avalanche.

Further south in Georgia, an earlier-than-expected call for Jon Ossoff buoyed Democratic hopes, followed by a pickup in the North Carolina Senate race, which saw Gov. Roy Cooper projected to defeat former Republican National Committee Chairman Michael Whatley. Democrats even put up a fight in ruby-red Florida’s statewide races, with David Jolly, the nominee for governor, and Angie Nixon, the democratic socialist Senate candidate, running ahead of their opponents until the conservative Panhandle’s votes were counted.

Then came South Carolina, where Darline Graham, who was foisted upon the state’s Republican Party by Donald Trump to succeed her brother, Sen. Lindsey Graham, after his death, went down to an historic defeat. The Palmetto State hasn’t seen a Democrat elected to the Senate since 1998.

Amy Acton, Ohio’s Democratic nominee for governor, routed her opponent Vivek Ramaswamy after he released a widely condemned ad that weaponized a horrific part of her childhood. In Texas, James Talarico is projected for a stunning win over scandal-plagued Ken Paxton, and Gina Hinojosa ousted the incumbent Greg Abbott from the governor’s mansion.

Other elections around the country — Senate races in Michigan, Kansas and Iowa; pivotal House contests and gubernatorial races — appear to be trending for Democrats.

For a decade, the party has wondered when, or if, a reckoning would arrive for Trump and MAGA. That moment has arrived.

Again and again, Republicans have followed the president over the cliff, no more so than during the past 22 months. In 2024, Trump expanded on the coalition that had carried him to success in 2016 by making inroads with Black men, Latinos and young voters. It was a success that, at least in part, led Republicans to launch a national gerrymandering scheme largely thwarted by Democrats, as well as an unfavorable economic climate of the president’s own making.

Want more sharp takes on politics? Sign up for our free newsletter, Standing Room Only, written by Amanda Marcotte, now also a weekly show on YouTube or wherever you get your podcasts.

But Trump also made the grievous error of attacking the very communities that helped return him to the White House. The combination of his assaults on voting rights and Black history, as well as the Supreme Court’s gutting of the Voting Rights Act, ensured that Black voters turned out overwhelmingly for Democrats. After more than a year of ICE raids, mass deportations and threats against naturalized citizens, Latinos have swung to the Democrats en masse, playing a decisive role in Talarico’s win. Trump’s attacks on civil rights, immigrants’ rights and the social safety net have cut across demographic groups, and have had a strong negative impact on young voters. Drunk on his own power, the president has spent the better part of two years splintering his own coalition — and giving Democrats an opening they were happy to exploit.

The monumental defeat suffered by Republicans offers a sobering lesson for Democrats — to not forget what brought them to this night of victory.

The message voters are sending is loud and clear: They want fighters, leaders who are willing to throw out conventional wisdom and do battle for working-class interests. That’s what Ossoff sensed last fall, when he was considered to be the nation’s most endangered Democrat. Instead of playing it safe, he staked his candidacy on affordability issues, particularly subsidies for the Affordable Care Act. In the midst of the longest government shutdown on record, Ossoff held firm: He would not vote to reopen the federal government unless the tax credits were restored.

Other Democratic candidates got the message. Dr. Abdul El-Sayed in Michigan proved steadfast in linking the billions in federal funding that continue to subsidize Israel’s ongoing assault on Gaza to skyrocketing consumer prices on the homefront. Another physician, Dr. Annie Andrews, who defeated Graham, unabashedly campaigned on creating a healthcare public option and gun control measures in South Carolina — and won.

Right now, Democrats are feeling the wind at their backs. But after the excitement fades from tonight’s successes, they will realize they are now facing an even bigger challenge: To stay true to the fragile mandate they have been given by voters, and to not, as they have done so often in the past, slip on the proverbial banana peel that is waiting for them.

Throughout his campaign for the Senate, Paxton was fond of saying, “If we lose Texas, we lose America.” Tonight, Democrats put Texas solidly in their column — and America is still standing.
