The Missing Critical Minerals Investment

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Since its founding in 1922, Foreign Affairs has been the leading forum for serious discussion of American foreign policy and global affairs. The magazine has featured contributions from many leading international affairs experts.

Tom Moerenhout is a Professor at Columbia University’s School of Public and International Affairs, Director of Critical Materials at Columbia University’s Center on Global Energy Policy, and Executive Director at the Geneva Platform for Resilient Value Chains.

Kevin Brunelli is a Nonresident Fellow at Columbia University’s Center on Global Energy Policy and previously served in the Office of Critical Minerals and Energy Supply Chains at the U.S. Department of State.

To Catch Up to China, America and Its Allies Must Dig

The United States and its allies are finally taking supply chains for critical minerals seriously. China has spent the last three decades building a dominant share of global mineral production, and the export controls Beijing has introduced periodically since 2023 have made clear just how dangerous it is for other countries to rely on China for access to materials that are essential components of semiconductors, defense technologies, and automobiles.

Washington and European capitals are now trying to minimize their exposure. Alongside private firms, these governments have begun investing in both upstream industries including mines, refineries, and recycling infrastructure and downstream industries such as magnets and batteries. The bulk of their efforts so far have focused on mines already in operation or close to it and on refining. It is true that the United States and Europe do not have sufficient facilities to process raw ore into specialized chemicals or refined materials and that building more refining facilities will help reduce their reliance on China and alleviate immediate supply chain concerns.

But this is a temporary fix. Refining facilities are valuable only if they have a steady, long-term supply of raw materials. That, in turn, requires new mines, especially as advanced industries demand ever more minerals. Because building a mine takes roughly 20 years, ensuring a long-term supply of minerals requires investing in new mines now. China, which is committed to maintaining its supply chain dominance, has been willing to allocate substantial capital to such ventures. Japan has, too, ever since China blocked exports of critical minerals to the country amid a dispute over the Senkaku Islands (known in China as the Diaoyu Islands) in 2010, which pushed Tokyo to start the long process of disentangling its own supply chains. But Japan still has a long way to go; after 15 years of investing, the country has reduced its dependence on China for rare earths from about 90 percent to between 65 and 70 percent—not nearly enough to supply its own needs, much less those of its allies.

In short, investing in refining is necessary but insufficient. To avoid future supply chain vulnerabilities, the United States and its allies, especially those in the G-7, must also start building a pipeline of new mines. These investments are long bets, and they are risky. But when successful, they reap high rewards.

The United States and its allies significantly outperform China in mineral exploration. Australian and Canadian firms alone account for roughly one-third of global exploration spending. But the vast majority of geological prospects never become operating mines. This is not for lack of financing in prospecting but because of the many financial disincentives and risks that appear once a mining project moves forward.

The initial stages of mining development rely chiefly on a unique ecosystem of mining companies called “juniors,” so called because they have a small market cap, focus exclusively on one or a few projects, and are nowhere large enough to list on major stock exchanges. Most of them list in Australia or Canada and concentrate on the initial high-risk exploration stages. Geological uncertainty makes betting on junior ventures extraordinarily risky, but if a mineral deposit proves to have significant reserves, the junior can sell the project or bring in a new partner, and that payout is large.

Yet in the next stages, investors tend to lose patience or run out of capital. Although initial prospecting is relatively cheap, subsequent feasibility studies that estimate a deposit’s size, engineering needs, costs, and expected returns can easily run into the tens of millions of dollars, in addition to the costs of drilling, engineering, permitting, and development planning. Commercial lenders stay away because these projects have no immediate revenue, no assets to offer as collateral, and no guarantees they will even be viable. The risk, in short, is too high to take for uncertain returns. Large mining companies also avoid these projects because they face pressure from shareholders to provide maximum returns. Even as demand for minerals has risen, the share of revenue that major mining companies have reinvested in new development has stayed flat.

Without outside financing, juniors cannot complete the studies needed to attract additional financing for construction, even if they have found a promising deposit. As single-asset companies, they have no other cash flow or assets to offer as collateral for additional loans. This moment is often referred to as the “valley of death.” Many companies looking for a way out of this predicament have turned to one reliable source of investment: China. Chinese companies are much more willing to take on this type of risk and provide the necessary capital to advance mining projects, and they are often the only viable option for juniors. Thus, China’s dominance in global supply chains relies on Western companies’ exploration. It endures not because China offers more competitive terms but because at a key financing stage, it is the only game in town.

China has been expanding its supply pipeline for critical minerals for decades. In 2025, it spent a record $32 billion on metals and mining projects through its Belt and Road Initiative, breaking the previous year’s record of $21 billion. Chinese firms are also willing to invest in equity, buying ownership stakes in companies that come with no guaranteed returns. Additional spending takes the form of resources-for-infrastructure deals, by which China funds infrastructure such as ports, energy facilities, and railways in mineral-rich countries in exchange for a share in the profits of mining projects—which Chinese companies and state banks may also help finance. By investing in a broad portfolio of mineral projects across several countries, Chinese firms can let the profits of those that succeed cover the losses of those that fail.

Apart from capital, the most important part of China’s mining strategy is know-how. Because failure is so common, the ability to identify deposits with the highest potential to deliver is crucial. Beijing relies on specialized state-owned enterprises, geological firms, private companies, and government officials to vet projects and work with host governments to secure permits, infrastructure, and delivery. The only G-7 country that maintains a comparable on-the-ground presence for mining projects is Japan, in countries such as Australia, Canada, Peru, and Uzbekistan. These projects are intended to secure critical minerals that can support Japan’s industries, but they are currently insufficient to meet all of Japan’s production needs, let alone broader allied needs.

The disparity between China’s mining investments and those of the United States and its allies is most evident in Africa, home to some of the largest untapped mineral resources. Whereas the United States invested about $300 million in critical mineral projects on the continent in 2023, China invested about $8 billion to $10 billion. Washington has recognized the need to close this gap, but it still has a long way to go.

In the Democratic Republic of the Congo, a particular focus of the Trump administration, Chinese companies control about 70 percent of cobalt and copper mines. Between 2008 and 2020, Chinese banks and companies extended over $9 billion in credit for mine development and operations in Congo, in addition to $3 billion in infrastructure loans from the Export-Import Bank of China. The United States and other G-7 countries had the opportunity to take part in these investments but judged the projects to be too risky. In 2015, for example, the Canadian-listed mining company Ivanhoe Mines sought funding for its Kamoa-Kakula copper project in Congo. With no Western investors willing to take on the risk, the Chinese firm Zijin Mining acquired an ownership stake. Today, Kamoa-Kakula is on track to become one of the largest copper mines in the world. Meanwhile, in 2016 and 2020, the only major American mining company active in Congo sold its stakes in two substantial copper-cobalt deposits, its only remaining stakes in the country, to a Chinese mining company.

The owners of a mine control where its minerals eventually go. When Chinese firms own a mine, the output flows either to their refineries or downstream manufacturers in China. The Chinese battery giant CATL, for example, has an ownership stake in the Kisanfu mine in Congo, where it secures a steady supply of copper and cobalt for its batteries. U.S. automakers and allied battery producers have also invested in some projects, but none to the same degree—even still, much of that material flows through China. The concentration of this refined material in China gives Beijing ever-greater market power and the ability to weaponize it.

In theory, the United States and its allies have the means of financing their own pipeline of future mining projects. In 2024, two Toronto-based stock exchanges hosted nearly 1,100 publicly listed mining companies, 40 percent of the global total. These companies account for roughly one-third of the world’s total mining equity capital raised over the last five years. About half of the listed companies’ projects were based in Canada. The Australian Stock Exchange is another key exchange, with about 850 companies listed in 2024 for mining development.

But these companies are facing pressures that discourage investment in promising projects. The world’s most accessible mineral deposits have already been tapped, so potential new deposits are often located in more complicated geological environments, making the early stages of exploration more difficult and costly. Moving from exploration to production is also taking more time, as increased social and environmental requirements draw out permitting processes. This lengthening timeline lowers private investors’ appetite for risk because they have to wait longer for their investments to start generating returns. At the same time, Chinese overproduction of critical minerals has depressed prices for key commodities. Despite strong signals that demand for the minerals will continue to increase, these low prices discourage new players from entering the game and weaken investors’ incentives to help those already in it.

U.S. and allied governments therefore need to draw in substantial new investment. The United States has made a start by taking equity stakes in domestic and international mining companies and expanding loans to them. Official announcements claim that these investments total around $50 billion, although the value of the deals that have been signed falls closer to $9 billion, and the money actually moving adds up to around $4 billion. Most of this money does not go to the projects languishing in the valley of death. Instead, the projects getting funded are those that are closer to production, because they can help tackle immediate supply risks.

This is a good start, but ventures that remain at early stages still need funding, and private investors are still wary of mining projects. Since 2025, the few institutional investors that have gotten involved tended to do so after the U.S. government announced an equity stake or loan to back a project. Although such caution is not new, relying on government signals and public investment sets up a perverse incentive for private companies not to invest in the absence of public support, which is exactly what governments want to avoid.

To really pull in private capital for exploration and mine development, the United States and the European Union should take a page from Canada’s book. The Canadian government offers tax credits to companies engaged in mineral exploration, and its “flow-through shares” system helps mining companies finance exploration and development by offering tax incentives to investors. If the rest of the G-7 can establish this kind of financial architecture, far more exploration capital would flow to critical mineral projects. And if member countries link their systems by letting investors claim tax benefits for exploration anywhere in the G-7, they could build the large, shared pool of exploration and development capital needed to support projects through to construction. In addition, G-7 countries must remove barriers for large institutional investors to enter the mining sector—primarily by reforming their tax codes, which currently disincentivize tax-exempt investors, such as pension funds, endowments, and foundations, from investing in mining projects because returns on such investments can be taxed as income.

Finally, when the incentives for private investment still do not line up for certain projects, G-7 countries should use public capital to directly help mining companies escape the valley of death. To do this, the United States, Japan, and European countries should set up a joint facility to pool financing for feasibility studies, which are particularly difficult to raise private funding for, establishing a broad portfolio so that the losses from any failures are covered by the winners. This pooled fund would allocate grants to promising projects in exchange for equity in the project or a royalty once the mine goes into production. Many projects would still fail, but with a dedicated, well-staffed facility, the quality of investments would likely improve—as they have for China, which takes a similar portfolio approach to early-stage mining. And the benefits to both investors and national interests would far outweigh the lost capital.

Today’s chokepoints for critical minerals sit mostly at the processing or refining stage, but future threats to the minerals needed for manufacturing, energy, artificial intelligence, and defense technology will be at the mines. G-7 countries have the markets and the policy tools to build a pipeline of secure mining projects and prevent the next economic emergency. Investment in refining facilities means little if the United States and its allies have no minerals to feed them a decade from now. The only choice, then, is to commit to financing new mining projects now.

Chainalysis Describes Recovery Efforts After Mercado Bitcoin Security Incident

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainalysis said on November 2 that it had supported tracing and containment efforts following a security incident involving Mercado Bitcoin. Incident estimates can differ between early reports, technical responders and the affected platform.

The difference between “identified”, “frozen” and “returned” is easy to lose in fast-moving security coverage. It matters to victims because only the last of those creates a completed recovery. Clear language also protects the credibility of investigators by avoiding a bigger success claim than the evidence supports.

Chainalysis said on November 2 that it had supported tracing and containment efforts following a security incident involving Mercado Bitcoin. The analytics company referred to an approximately $49 million incident and said nearly $10 million had been frozen with help from partners. These figures describe the company’s account of tracing and freezes, not a final audited loss statement or confirmed reimbursement to every customer. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Blockchain analytics can reconstruct movements between addresses and help investigators identify where suspected proceeds arrive. When funds reach a service with intervention powers, the operator may be able to freeze a balance or respond to a legal request. But tracing a token, freezing it and returning its value are three separate operations involving different evidence and legal approvals.

The response illustrates why cryptocurrency incidents increasingly depend on cooperation between issuers, investigators and exchanges. Decentralised settlement can be fast, while preservation of assets may require centralised intervention at a service endpoint. That tension is not new, but it becomes visible when investigators publish how much suspected value they could immobilise.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Incident estimates can differ between early reports, technical responders and the affected platform. Figures should therefore be attributed and not blended into a supposedly exact final loss. A freeze does not establish the funds can be released immediately, and analysts should not equate linked addresses with a legal finding against every downstream wallet holder.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Follow official updates for verified loss totals, recovery procedures and whether the affected platform changes its custody or operational controls. For users, the practical questions are whether balances remain accessible, how claims will be handled and what evidence supports any reimbursement promise. A transparent accounting of outcomes is more important than a dramatic seizure headline.

It's a huge week in gaming with the highly anticipated launch of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time for the Switch 2. The first review from the famous Japanese outlet Famitsu has now officially gone live. So, what did it think of Nintendo's remake?

If you aren't familiar with this publication's unique scoring system, four critics each deliver a score out of ten, and these scores are then totalled. For Ocarina of Time on the Switch 2, it's received 9/9/9/10, giving it an overall score of 37/40. So, it's been well received by the outlet!

In case you need a reminder, the original 1998 Nintendo 64 game was awarded a "perfect" score of 40/40. As for the 2011 3DS release, it was also given a score of 37/40. We'll be delivering our own final thoughts here on Nintendo Life later today, and be on the lookout for a review round up.

Booking.com Bets Travelers Will Research With AI but Book With It

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Photo Credit: Laura Houldsworth, Booking.com's managing director, Asia Pacific, at Skift Asia Forum in Thailand April 29, 2026. Skift

AI may own the start of the trip, but Booking.com is betting trust and payments decide who gets the booking. Its experience with superapps, where daily use never became travel spending, suggests generative chatbots may face the same problem.

Booking.com is okay with travelers starting their trip planning with AI. It just wants to be the platform to close things.

Travelers are increasingly researching trips with AI, but that doesn’t mean the booking follows. Booking.com frames its challenge in Asia-Pacific as staying relevant at the moment people are ready to pay, wherever they may begin.

A Booking.com survey conducted earlier this year found that 64% of travelers in the region use AI to plan trips. Yet Booking Holdings, its parent company, has said recently that traffic referred directly by large language models is rare, representing well under 1% of room nights.

Laura Houldsworth, Booking.com’s vice president for Asia-Pacific, told Skift the gap exists because travelers don’t stay in one chatbot. “They might be searching on Claude one minute, ChatGPT another, Gemini, then rounding out with Google, and then coming to us directly,” she said. Decision intelligence for travel leaders.

Booking.com's Asia-Pacific chief, Laura Houldsworth, told Skift the company is comfortable with travelers starting trip research inside AI chatbots as long as the booking still closes on Booking.com. A Booking.com survey earlier this year found 64% of travelers in Asia-Pacific use AI to plan trips, while parent Booking Holdings has said traffic referred directly by large language models accounts for well under 1% of room nights. Houldsworth attributes the gap to travelers moving between Claude, ChatGPT, Gemini, and Google before coming to Booking.com directly, so no single assistant gets credit. Her argument is that trust decides where the money lands — 400 million reviews, secure payments, and customer service that chatbots don't offer. The same logic shapes her view of superapps: daily usage hasn't translated into big-ticket travel spend, so Booking.com is plugging into platforms travelers already use rather than competing with them, starting with WhatsApp as a booking channel in India. Houldsworth also called intra-Asia the fastest-growing travel corridor in the world, with domestic room nights in Asia growing at a low double-digit rate against mid-single-digit growth companywide, which has cushioned softness in the Europe–Asia corridor tied to Middle East conflict. Alternative accommodation is about 37% of global room nights, with regulation the main risk, and the connected trip strategy depends on local payments landing first.

Autonomous site reliability engineering startup Traversal bags $120M in funding at a $1B valuation

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Traversal Inc., a startup that’s trying to automate site reliability engineering tasks with artificial intelligence agents, seems to be closing in on that goal after raising $120 million in fresh funding, bringing its valuation to $1 billion.

The newly minted unicorn’s round was led by Kleiner Perkins, Redpoint Ventures and Khosla Ventures, three of Silicon Valley’s highest-profile venture capital firms, whose names should give it the credibility it needs to accelerate its growth plans. Sequoia Capital – another big VC name – also participated in the round, alongside Battery Ventures and Amex Ventures.

AI might have made the job of software developers much easier than it used to be, but for the site reliability engineers tasked with maintaining the infrastructure to run that code, it has only made life much more difficult. The influx of AI-generated code has put infrastructure engineers under intense pressure, and they’re struggling to keep up.

According to a report earlier this year by Splunk Inc. and Oxford Economics, enterprise downtime costs have risen more than 50% in the last two years, draining up to $600 billion annually from businesses worldwide. That’s because software developers have increased their code output by around 180% over the same timeframe, which means those poor infrastructure engineers are forced to work harder than ever to match their growing output.

Enter Traversal, which is coming to the rescue with a highly promising autonomous site reliability engineer that’s designed to assist human specialists by diagnosing, remediating and preventing information technology infrastructure problems. Instead of just keeping watch and firing off alerts to human engineers when an outage occurs, Traversal actively investigates any issues that pop up and does its best to fix them so as to ease their ungodly workload.

Co-founder and Chief Executive Anish Agarwal said there’s an urgent need for a specialized AI site reliability engineer, because not even the best general-purpose large language models have been able to show they’re up to the task. He points to the ORCA-bench benchmark that his company co-developed alongside researchers from Cornell Tech and Columbia University. It reveals that even the most advanced frontier models barely achieve 25% accuracy on medium-difficulty SRE tasks, with their hallucination rates often exceeding 40%.

“Site reliability engineering is one of the hardest problems in applied AI because every company’s software environment is different, and with downtime costing companies as much as $600 billion a year, the stakes are clearly enormous,” Agarwal said.

Traversal’s AI far surpasses what the best frontier models can do. It’s designed to be deployed in enterprises’ IT environments, where it sits and analyzes massive volumes of operational telemetry, including logs, metrics and traces, so as to establish a baseline for normal behavior. When problems occur, it uses causal machine learning to identify the root cause of the issue, and then works out a solution so all the human engineer has to do is accept its recommendation to restore normal service.

Agarwal’s background in causal machine learning is the key to Traversal’s AI SRE’s much higher accuracy. It’s an AI discipline that combines causal inference with machine learning to identify cause-and-effect relationships, rather than relying on statistical correlations. In essence, it enables Traversal’s models to simulate the effects of various actions, interventions and policy changes have on IT infrastructure, so it can quickly work out what the likely cause of a problem is.

Agarwal said the company is striving to build a “superintelligent AI SRE” that can finally help enterprises to keep pace with the rapid velocity of their AI-enabled developer teams. And he believes it’s well on the way to achieving that goal. “After two years refining our technology, we’re seeing customers expand how they use Traversal across increasingly complex production environments,” he said.

In the last year, Traversal has grown its revenue more than nine-fold and its number of monthly active users has multiplied by 20. Some of its best-known customers include Pepsi Co., DigitalOcean Holdings Inc., Nubank and Harvey AI Corp.

Traversal’s momentum is impressive, but Agarwal said the company is only just getting started. With $120 million in the bank, it’s going to accelerate hiring within its engineering, go-to-market and research and development teams so it can get closer to its goal of building a superintelligent AI SRE.

Kleiner Perkins’ Aditya Naganath said Traversal is the first company he has seen that brings “real intelligence” into complex production environments, and he’s confident there’s going to be enormous demand for it. “As AI dramatically accelerates the pace at which software is written and shipped, the complexity of managing production will only grow,” he predicted. “Traversal gives engineering teams the intelligence they need to understand their systems, resolve incidents faster and operate with confidence at scales that simply weren’t possible before.”

2026 season could highlight the unfairness of the eight-division structure

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

From time to time, the NFL’s current structure has created unfair results, with the best of four bad teams assigned to a predetermined division earning a playoff berth and the home game that goes along with it.

This year, the NFC has two very strong divisions, one so-so division, and one very weak division.

Here’s the basic reality. With the four NFC division winners getting tickets to the playoff party, the wild-card berths will go to three teams. And, currently, there are three teams in the NFC North and two in the NFC West that are potentially better than whoever will win the NFC East and the NFC South.

Between the Vikings, Bears, Lions, Packers, 49ers, Seahawks, and Rams, two will be crowned division winners. That leaves five for the wild-card berths.

Ultimately, two of them will be frozen out of the playoffs — even if both of those teams end up with better records than the best teams of the NFC East and NFC South.

During last Friday’s episode of PFT Live, I threw out an alternative approach that would be far more fair to all teams.

Instead of two conferences with a quartet of four-team divisions, why not pivot to four eight-team divisions?

NFL East, NFL North, NFL South, NFL West. The easiest configuration would consist of slapping the current East, North, South, and West divisions into one conference each. While some tweaks could be needed to better align things geographically, it would be far more fair to guarantee a playoff spot (and a home game) to the best of eight teams, not the best of four teams.

Decisions would have to be made about scheduling. The home-and-home series (a vestige of the days when there were far fewer teams) would need to be retired. Each team would play the seven other teams in its conference, with 10 (or 11, inevitably) other games.

The playoffs would consist of the four conference champions (with the best two getting byes) and 10 wild-card teams. And there would be an added benefit — it would be possble to have the best two teams meet in the Super Bowl, without regard to the traditional AFL-NFL/AFC-NFC separation.

It would be one league. One playoff tree. Any combination of teams would, in theory, be possible for the Super Bowl. When the dust settles on the semifinal round, the best two teams would remain.

That said, it won’t be easy to get the owners to give up the current format, which entails in any given year a one-in-four shot at hosting a playoff game. But if the goal is to constantly strive for ways to create more compelling matchups, an eight-team, four-conference approach makes a lot more sense than the current structure.

Football is a meritocracy. A configuration of four, eight-team conferences would be far more conducive to that ideal.

Will it happen? Probably not. Should it be taken seriously by the league? Absolutely.

Maybe this year, when the NFC East and NFC South provide none-of-the-above champions and two very viable teams are sent home after Week 18, will spark a real conversation about meaningful change.

Cerillion 26.2 Extends AI From Customer Care to Churn Prediction

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Cerillion announced the general availability of Cerillion 26.2 on November 4, 2026, adding AI capabilities to its pre-integrated BSS/OSS Suite for communications service providers. The release spans customer care, service configuration, competitor-aware package design and churn prediction.

The additions reach across work usually divided among support, product and retention teams. They sit inside the pre-integrated suite, alongside the catalogue, promotions and configuration workflows they are intended to support, while central governance and connections to outside AI systems form the deployment layer beneath them.

For customer support, the new Customer Care Agent retrieves the customer and service details needed during an enquiry and recommends how to proceed. Cerillion says the agent is intended to make support faster and more consistent while reducing the training needed to bring new users onto the system.

A second agent handles a broader range of complex service and system configuration work. Cerillion says the Service Configuration Agent reduces hands-on effort and dependence on specialist knowledge, accelerating the introduction of new services and network integrations.

Product teams can also use information about competing offers when the catalogue’s agentic tools design packages. Cerillion says Competitor-aware Package Creation is intended to speed responses to market changes and help differentiate the resulting propositions within Enterprise Product Catalogue.

Predictive Churn applies AI and machine-learning models to identify customers most at risk of leaving. Cerillion said the models proved highly effective in customer pilots; their scores provide earlier insight into retention risk and support targeted campaigns and offers through the Promotions Engine.

The release’s agentic functions draw on the AI Management Centre, Model Context Protocol (MCP) Server and Agent2Agent Server. The management centre governs models centrally, while the MCP Server provides controlled access to Cerillion’s Open APIs through Progressive Discovery. The Agent2Agent Server coordinates multi-step workflows across agents and systems. Operators can adopt Cerillion’s AI, integrate existing in-house or third-party AI investments, or combine the approaches.

The company’s AI Hub sets out how outside systems join that environment. Approved external agents can discover and invoke Cerillion capabilities through Open APIs and ODA with scoped permissions and human approvals where required. Operators decide which tools and data agents can access, with policy-based approvals and audit trails governing execution. External model options include ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Azure AI.

The architecture predates this release. Cerillion 26.1, announced April 29, 2026, introduced communication and coordination between agents, building on AI agents and the MCP Server from the previous release. Its Progressive Discovery design lets agents locate relevant data and services as workflows develop, providing shared context for coordinated tasks across systems.

The release also changes core operational products. Enterprise Product Catalogue’s Dynamic Catalogue Workspace presents complex product, package and pricing structures in a grid so product teams can compare and update them at scale, applying edits directly within that view.

Convergent Charging System adds a native Nchf interface for 5G standalone charging. REST-based and Diameter charging interfaces can coexist in the same deployment, allowing operators to migrate towards 5G standalone while continuing to support hybrid networks.

Enterprise Product Catalogue has received TM Forum ODA Component Certification for Product Catalog Management. Cerillion has added 15 TM Forum Real World Open API certifications since 26.1, taking its total to 47 across seven customers. The company says that is more than any other independent BSS/OSS vendor.

For operators, the release’s practical value rests on governed execution across that environment. The AI additions widen the work that can be automated, while permissions, human approvals and audit trails define how that automation is controlled across Cerillion and external systems.

Mount Sinai Chooses Sectra’s AI-Assisted Radiology Reporting

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Sectra announced on November 4, 2026, a seven-year agreement to provide its AI-assisted Sectra Reporting module in Sectra One Cloud across all Mount Sinai Health System radiology services, covering more than 1.2 million exams annually. Go-live at the New York health system is planned for spring 2027.

Sectra described Mount Sinai as the first US health system to partner with it for radiology reporting. The agreement extends an existing relationship: Mount Sinai already uses Sectra’s enterprise imaging for radiology, breast imaging and enterprise archive. Adding reporting to that installation is intended to reduce vendor complexity and simplify IT management.

The deployment is designed to take radiologists from worklist to signed report without moving between separate imaging and reporting systems. They can dictate freely while the software structures the report, review an AI-drafted impression and receive quality checks that flag inconsistencies before sign-off. Shorter turnaround is the objective; with go-live still ahead, the announcement describes a planned workflow rather than outcomes already recorded at Mount Sinai.

The product documentation describes a one-click AI tool that summarizes and organizes findings from free-form dictation into an impression. Radiologists can also use speech input, rich-text templates and examination-specific forms; capture measurements and lesion tracking; and embed links to image sets in the report. Once a report is signed, Sectra UniView can present it with images and measurements to referring physicians, while the reporting workflow can automatically load the next case.

Sectra reports 14,000 active Sectra Reporting users worldwide, 27 million reports per year and use in 21 countries. Those figures apply to the wider product footprint rather than Mount Sinai’s deployment, showing that the module is already in use elsewhere as it enters this large US health system.

Sectra introduced Sectra Reporting to the US market on November 26, 2025, with automated impression generation, integration of external AI results and deep links to measurements, annotations and lesion-tracking data. The company maintains support for third-party reporting products, allowing customers to choose its native module while retaining other supported reporting systems.

Mount Sinai brings substantial scale to that approach, with seven hospitals, more than 400 outpatient practices, over 600 research and clinical laboratories and the Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai. Dr. David S. Mendelson, its Vice Chair of Radiology Information Technology, said integrating reporting with the imaging platform allows the two to evolve together and brings new capabilities, including AI, to radiologists “without yet another system to integrate.”

Isaac Zaworski, President of Sectra, Inc., characterized the adoption as part of a broader shift toward unified diagnostic workflows, with fewer applications for radiologists and faster turnaround for patients as the anticipated benefits.

For Mount Sinai, the practical test will be whether that consolidation works in daily practice: radiologists moving from examination to checked, signed report in one workflow, while the IT organization manages reporting within the imaging platform it already operates. The agreement puts enterprise integration at the center of the deployment, with AI embedded in report preparation and shorter turnaround still to be demonstrated after implementation.

The growing role of crypto in online casinos

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Online gaming is no longer limited to playing a game through a browser or downloading an app. Players now have access to multiplayer platforms, mobile games, subscription services, and online casinos, each offering different ways to play and interact. In particular, online casinos have expanded their gaming libraries, offering users a plethora of table games and online slots, as well as live dealer sessions and casino originals. Payment methods on these platforms have also changed. Bank cards and digital wallets remain widely used, but cryptocurrency has become another option for online services that support digital asset payments.

Bitcoin, Ethereum, and stablecoins such as USDT are now accepted by a number of online casino operators, allowing players to use digital assets for deposits and, depending on the platform, withdrawals. For players interested in digital currency gaming, BetUS Casino offers an online destination to explore and play casino games with crypto. Cryptocurrency in this space also raises practical questions about how transactions are processed, which platforms support which assets, and what players should consider before choosing an online casino.

Better internet, mobile tech, and cloud services have changed how online gaming works. Faster connections make real-time multiplayer games easier to play, and cloud services help platforms update games, manage accounts, and let people play on different devices. Now, games can get new content and features without players needing to download a whole new version. Buying digital content is also common on many platforms. These changes have greatly influenced a player’s expectations, especially around easy access and account management.

Online casinos have grown with these changes. Many now offer hundreds or even thousands of games, including slots, table games, and live dealer options, all available on desktop and mobile. Operators also handle digital services like player accounts, game purchases, deposits, and withdrawals. This has created an online space where playing games and paying for them are closely linked.

Cryptocurrency is becoming more common for online payments, giving platforms another way to accept money from users who already have digital assets. Unlike card payments, which go through banks and payment networks, crypto payments move assets directly between blockchain addresses. The kind of cryptocurrency matters too. Bitcoin and Ethereum are well known, but their prices can change quickly, which might affect how much a deposit is worth if the funds stay in those assets. Stablecoins like USDT are different because they are meant to keep a value close to the US dollar. This makes them helpful for people who want to pay digitally without as much price risk, though they still have some risks of their own.

Making a crypto deposit involves more than simply entering a payment amount. The platform or its payment provider supplies a wallet address and specifies which blockchain network to use. Users need to follow these instructions carefully, as assets such as USDT are available across multiple networks that may not be compatible with one another. Once funds are sent, the transaction is verified and recorded on the relevant blockchain. The receiving platform may require a set number of network confirmations before crediting the account. Transaction fees and processing times also vary depending on the asset, network activity, and the platform’s own deposit procedures.

Cryptocurrency and online casinos are connected in more ways than just adding Bitcoin or USDT as payment options. Some casinos are built specifically for users with digital assets, so their account balances, deposits, and withdrawals are set up for crypto transactions. Others add crypto tokens to their existing payment systems, which already include bank cards and digital wallets. For players, these differences can change how casinos manage funds and which currencies are available. Also, not every casino treats crypto the same way. Some may let you deposit with a certain cryptocurrency but not withdraw with it.

Online casino users now have more ways to pay, but other things still set casinos apart. They still compete on the games they offer, the software they use, mobile access, withdrawal rules, and customer support. Accepting several cryptocurrencies might attract people who use digital assets, but that doesn’t tell potential players much about the casino’s overall quality. Payment terms, game selection, and withdrawal conditions are still important.

As more online casinos start accepting cryptocurrency, players now have more choices than ever. But payment methods are just one factor to think about. The selection of games can be very different from one casino to another. Most well-known casinos have slots, table games, and live dealer options, but the number of games and the companies that make them can vary. Some casinos even create their own games or offer exclusive titles you won’t find elsewhere. For example, BetUS Casino has over 700 games with more games in the pipeline awaiting their launch, so players have plenty to try.

Casinos also use promotions to bring in new players, offering things like deposit bonuses, free bets, and other rewards. BetUS, for example, has several types of bonuses. One is a 150% Sportsbook bonus, which offers up to $750 across a player’s first three deposits, with a $50 minimum deposit and a 10x rollover requirement in sports. These offers can look appealing, but the attached conditions matter as much as the advertised rewards. Players should also consider licensing, account security, withdrawal policies, and customer support when comparing platforms. Casino ratings and independent reviews can help narrow options, but players should consider them alongside these practical details rather than treating them as a guarantee of quality.

Crypto payments let players control how they move their money, but it also places greater responsibility on the person making the transaction. Anyone using a personal wallet should protect their private keys and recovery phrases, as losing access to them can mean losing access to their funds. It is equally important to check the receiving address and blockchain network before confirming a transfer. Crypto transactions generally cannot be reversed once confirmed, so sending funds to an incorrect address or through an unsupported network can make recovery difficult or impossible. Players should also account for network fees, which can change with demand, and confirmation times, which may affect when a casino credits a deposit or processes a withdrawal.

It’s just as important to check out the casino itself. Just because a casino accepts Bitcoin, Ethereum, or USDT doesn’t mean it’s licensed or allowed to operate everywhere. Players should check which regulatory authority issued its licence, whether that licence covers online gambling, and whether the operator is permitted to accept customers from their jurisdiction. They should also understand the platform’s withdrawal procedures, including any limits, verification requirements, processing conditions, or restrictions on cryptocurrency withdrawals, before depositing funds.

Crypto gaming, sometimes called Web3 or blockchain gaming, means playing online games or using entertainment platforms that use cryptocurrencies or blockchain technology. This includes online casino games that accept digital currencies and games where players can own or trade digital items.

Players usually send cryptocurrency from their own wallet or an exchange to the casino’s deposit address. Once the transaction is confirmed on the blockchain, the casino adds the funds to the player’s account. Withdrawing works in much the same way.

Many online casinos accept cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin and Ethereum. Some also support Litecoin and stablecoins such as USDT. Since payment options vary, it’s a good idea to check which coins and networks each casino supports.

Sean "Diddy" Combs' Prison Menu for 57th Birthday Revealed

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Sean "Diddy" Combs will have access to hot oatmeal for breakfast, a burger for lunch and pork roast for dinner on his 57th birthday Nov. 4, according to a prison menu.

Sean "Diddy" Combs' 57th birthday menu has been set.

As the rapper continues to serve out his 50-month prison sentence on prostitution-related charges, he'll ring in the milestone with three square meals at Pennsylvania's FCI Allenwood Low on Nov. 4.

For breakfast, Combs and other inmates at the low-security federal prison will be served hot oatmeal, scrambled eggs, fruit, whole wheat bread with jelly and whole milk, according to a menu obtained by Us Weekly.

While a choice of hamburger or black bean burger with toppings—including lettuce, tomato, sliced red onions and condiments—is provided for lunch, dinner will be pork roast, mashed potatoes with gravy and mixed vegetables served with whole wheat bread.

And Combs will likely start his birthday bright and early. After all, an admissions handbook for FCI Allenwood Low obtained by E! News stated that breakfast runs from 6 a.m. to 7 a.m. at the dining hall, with lunch scheduled for 10:45 a.m. and dinner set to begin following the 4 p.m. inmate count.

Though prison rules prohibit food from being removed from the dining hall, snacks can be purchased from the commissary should Combs miss meal times. In fact, a three-pack of birthday cupcakes is available for $1.25, according to a commissary order form.

Combs was transferred to FCI Allenwood Low last month after sustaining a head injury while incarcerated at New Jersey's FCI Fort Dix, where he spent his 56th birthday in 2025.

"His transfer from one low-security facility to another is an important distinction, and while we cannot speak to the BOP's specific reasons for the move, it is consistent with a decision made with safety and security in mind,” Combs’ rep told E! in an Oct. 16 statement. "We are grateful for the attention being given to his well-being and hope he can continue serving his time without unnecessary disruption."

The Bad boy Records founder's prison release date is currently scheduled for Feb. 6, 2028.

Sean "Diddy" Combs Sentenced to 50 Months in Prison

The judge sentenced him to 50 months in prison—totaling over four years—plus five years of supervised release and a fine of $500,000 at the hearing Oct. 3, 2025.

The musician spoke out for the first time in court at his sentencing Oct. 3, apologizing to victims Cassie Ventura and "Jane."

"Domestic violence will always be a heavy burden that I will have to forever carry," Combs said. "My actions were disgusting, shameful and sick. I was sick, sick from the drugs, I was out of control, I needed help and I didn't get the help, and I cannot make no excuse."

He went on to share how the case has impacted him.

“I am just a human being, I was trying my best, I got lost in my excess and lost in my ego," he continued. "Because of my decision, I lost my freedom and the opportunity to raise my children and be there for my mother. I lost all of my businesses and lost my career and destroyed my reputation and, most of all, I lost my self-respect. I have been humbled and broken to my core. I hate myself right now. I’ve been stripped down to nothing."

In July, Judge Arun Subramanian scheduled Combs' sentencing date for Oct. 3, 2025. Subramanian denied Combs' request to be released on bond ahead of the sentencing, citing the rapper's "years-long pattern of violence" as a reason for him to remain behind bars.

Ex-Girlfriend Cassie Ventura's Lawyer Reacts to Verdict

After Combs was found not guilty of racketeering conspiracy as well as sex trafficking ex Cassie and ex "Jane"—though he was found guilty of transportation to engage in prostitution of both women—the singer's lawyer reacted to the verdicts.

"This entire criminal process started when our client Cassie Ventura had the courage to file her civil complaint in November 2023,” Douglas Wigdor said in a July 2 statement to E! News. “Although the jury did not find Combs guilty of sex trafficking Cassie beyond a reasonable doubt, she paved the way for a jury to find him guilty of transportation to engage in prostitution. By coming forward with her experience, Cassie has left an indelible mark on both the entertainment industry and the fight for justice."

Noting the charges—which Combs pleaded not guilty to—Wigdor added, "We must repeat – with no reservation – that we believe and support our client who showed exemplary courage throughout this trial.”

Wigdor also praised Ventura, who was one of 34 witnesses called to the standing during Combs' 29-day trial, for her effort.

“She displayed unquestionable strength and brought attention to the realities of powerful men in our orbit and the misconduct that has persisted for decades without repercussion,” he continued. “This case proved that change is long overdue, and we will continue to fight on behalf of survivors."

Sean "Diddy" Combs Reacts to Split Verdict

Upon hearing that he was acquitted on three of the five charges in his federal court case, Combs—who faced life in prison if convicted on all charges—got on his knees and placed his head down in a chair, in a prayer-like position, according to NBC News reporters in the courtroom.

Per the news organization, Combs' family stood in the courtroom, clapping and cheering for the music mogul's fate. The defense attorneys hugged each other, while Combs hugged lawyer Brian Steel.

Jurors Reach a Verdict in Sean "Diddy" Combs Sex Trafficking Case

On July 2, the 12-member jury delivered their verdicts on the five federal charges Combs faced in his sex trafficking trial. The jurors, who were on their third day of deliberation, found him guilty of two charges of transportation to engage in prostitution, relating to ex-girlfriends Ventura and a woman referred to as "Jane."

Combs—who had pleaded not guilty to all charges—was found not guilty of one count of racketeering conspiracy and two counts of sex trafficking.

The day before delivering their verdicts, the jury told U.S. District Judge Arun Subramanian they were unable to reach a consensus on the racketeering charge due to “unpersuadable views.” Subramanian addressed the jurors and encouraged them to continue their deliberations. A verdict was reached hours after deliberations began the next day.

Prosecutors Conclude With Nearly Five-Hour Closing Arguments

Prosecutor Christy Slavik spent four hours and 50 minutes delivering closing remarks to jurors, in which she argued that Sean “Diddy” Combs “used power, violence and fear to get what he wanted.”

“He doesn’t take no for an answer,” she said. “It was his kingdom. Everyone was there to serve him.”

Slavik pointed to Combs’ 2016 physical assault of his then-girlfriend Cassie Ventura and a 2024 alleged assault of his then-girlfriend, who has been going by “Jane” for the trial.

“These two incidents are separated by eight years, but they're not separate stories,” she said. “They're chapters in the same book, the story of Sean Combs and the criminal enterprise he led made up of his inner circle.”

“Again and again, that criminal enterprise serviced the defendant's every desire through a methodical pattern of violence, coercion and manipulation,” she continued. “The defendant counted on silence and shame to keep his crimes hidden. He thought that his fame, wealth and power put him above the law.”

Slavik argued that this trial Combs had the help of what she described as a “a small army of personal staff, who made it their mission to meet the defendant's every desire,  promote his power and protect his reputation at all costs.”

Prosecutors Seek to Streamline Charges Against Sean “Diddy” Combs

The prosecution submitted a filing to court, in which they requested to amend parts of their theories of attempted arson and kidnapping.

Although Combs is still facing the same racketeering conspiracy and sex trafficking counts—which include the kidnapping and arson allegations—prosecutors wrote in the June 24 letter they had “removed instructions from the charge relating to attempted kidnapping under both California and New York law, attempted arson under California law, and aiding and abetting sex trafficking.”

The government added that it “hopes” simplifying the language “not only serves the purposes of streamlining the instructions but also will avoid any confusion.”

Sean “Diddy” Combs Confirms He Won’t Be Testifying

Combs told Judge Subramanian that he will not take the stand during his sex trafficking trial.

The judge emphasized that regardless of the rap mogul’s decision, “no one, including the jury, could draw any inference or suggestion of your guilt from the fact that you did not testify.”

Combs, who confirmed that he spoke with his team about not testifying, emphasized that this was “solely” his decision.

"We have discussed it thoroughly, that is my decision," he said to the judge. "That is totally my decision, I am making it."

Defense Shares Estimation on Closing Arguments

Combs' defense attorney Teny Garagos told the judge that her team and the prosecution expect to take “at least four hours” each for their closing arguments.

With Homeland Security Investigations Special Agent Joseph Cerciello completing his multi-day testimony as the prosecution’s witness June 24, the defense will begin their argument. Combs’ team shared that they will not be moving forward with any witnesses and will instead focus on entered evidence.

Juror Sees Apparent Footage From “Freak Offs”

During Ceriello’s testimony June 23, the jury was shown explicit videos—which, according to NBC News reporters in the courtroom, appeared to be from Combs’ “freak offs”—that were entered into evidence.

While the special agent noted there were 50 explicit videos from 2021 and 2022, jurors were shown two files using headphones and screens.

Hotel Rooms Were Stocked With Baby Oil, Plan-B Pills For “Freak Offs”

In voicemails to his former chief of staff Kristina Khorram played June 23, Combs detailed a specific list of requests while staying at a hotel in November 2021, including a restock on baby oil—which he regularly used during “freak offs.”

“Tell them to call me before they are on the way,” he said to Khorram. “Can’t believe I’m out. Put 20 bottles in this motherf--ker.”

Along with Khorram extending the hotel visit for an additional day, she replied, “OK. The baby oil is outside the door.”

In text messages, his former employee texted that she was preparing an IV drip for him and Jane, which he used as part of at least one “freak off” recovery, according to the group text messages.

She also made sure that there were Plan-B pills, an emergency contraceptive, in his nightstand.

Cassie Ventura and Sean “Diddy” Combs’s Texts About His Alleged Abuse Revealed

Jurors were shown a series of text messages between Combs and Ventura, during which the “Long Way 2 Go” singer candidly detailed the toll his alleged abuse was taking on her.

After Combs asked whether Ventura, whom he dated on and off from 2007 to 2018, wanted to take a break in March 2017, she replied, “No. I just don't want to be beat down for being defiant or ever. You treat me and make me feel like I don't matter."

In another instance, she further accused her ex of physically assaulting her.

“I give you love, and as soon as I turn my head for a second and you get fucked up, you drag me down the hall by my hair. I'm 30 years old,” Ventura wrote to him that May. “This isn't play anymore. I felt like I was dead last night and it wasn't happening to me bc seeing my light was so beautiful."

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Former Assistant Denies Being Drug Mule But Says He Bought Thousands of Dollars in Drugs for the Rapper

Combs’ former assistant Brendan Paul—whom he employed from 2022 to 2024—testified that he had purchased drugs for his boss on multiple occasions, including “Marijuana, 2C, cocaine, ketamine, ecstasy.”

“We would text the drug dealer and then the drug dealer would come to either of the homes,” Paul—who also recalled being fired multiple times for varying offenses, including forgetting a Lululemon fanny pack—said of the process. “I was introduced to them either through texts or phone calls.”

However, Paul vehemently denied being a “drug mule” for the rap mogul.

Judge Cancels Court on Day 26 of Sean “Diddy” Combs Trial Over Sick Juror

Judge Subramanian adjourned the courtroom for the day on June 18 after a member of the main jury did not return due to illness. (Court is not in session on June 19 for Juneteenth.)

“We have a sick juror who cannot be here and had vertigo and had to turn back,” the judge said. “The juror's partner notified us. He is not here, and he is on the regular jury, so it appears we cannot go on today.”

The judge noted that it’s unclear whether the juror’s vertigo flare-up—which causes dizziness, nausea, and headaches, along with other symptoms—is temporary or a long-term issue, but would “monitor the situation.”

With court not in session June 19 for Juneteenth, Judge Subramanian said the trial would resume with a shortened session on June 20, running from 9 a.m. to 1 p.m.

Second Sean “Diddy” Combs Jury Member Faces Possible Dismissal

Shortly after Judge Subramanian dismissed Juror No. 6 over conflicting answers as to his place of residence, a second member faces possible dismissal over an alleged improper conversation about the case with a former colleague. The member in question was questioned by the judge and handed his phone in for inspection, per NBC News.

A decision had not been made as to the member’s dismissal by the end of the court session on June 16.

Kanye West Supports Sean “Diddy” Combs With Courthouse Visit

Kanye West arrived at the New York City courthouse June 13 in support of Combs. The “Stronger” rapper, who was in the building for about 30 minutes, made a brief visit to the overflow area but never entered the courtroom. The main room is reserved for Combs’ family and legal team, as well as the media and spectators who wait in line for hours for a seat.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Says Mogul Was Upset After She Attended Another Man’s “Freak Off”

Jane recalled an argument between them after she went on a trip to Las Vegas with another rapper and his wife.

She testified that it led to sexual voyeurism after she solicited Anton, a sex worker whom she and Combs often hired, where a group of people watched him have sex with a woman in a hotel room during the trip.

Jane told Combs’ attorney Geragos that the rap mogul was upset after accusing her of attending another man’s “freak off.”

Prosecutors Seek Removal of Juror in Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Trial

On day 21 of Combs' trial, assistant U.S. attorney Maurene Comey shared her team is seeking to remove one of the 12 members of the jury.

“We were very reluctant to put in this letter,” she told Judge Subramanian before the jury entered the New York City courtroom. “It appeared to be a lack of candor with the court that raises serious issues with us.”

Defense attorney Alexandra Shapiro—who said that Juror No. 6 is among the panel’s Black members—asked for one or two days to “respond in writing” and “go over case law.”

In response, Judge Subramanian said he will “reserve on handling logistics” about the juror until a decision is made.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Has Heated Exchange With Defense Team During Cross-Examination

Jane and Garagos had a tense conversation June 10 as the latter insinuated the witness was having sex with men for luxury goods.

Geragos went through text messages from November 2021 between Jane and Combs, in which the witness was upset that the rap mogul was having sex with other women. Per the texts, she asked Combs if he gave the other woman “a Chanel bag” after having sex.

Jane said that she didn’t get a Chanel purse from Combs, telling Garagos that she “got trauma.”

When the attorney asked what a Bottega bag was, she replied, “I’m sure you have one.”

And when Geragos asked how much the item costs, Jane asked, “How much does my body cost?”

Jane was granted a 10-minute break following the exchange.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Says She Wondered What Was “Driving Him” Sexually

Jane testified that around 2022, she believed that Combs was a “cuckhold,” a person who finds pleasure in watching their partner be physically intimate with another person, after looking up her and Combs’ sexual dynamic.

“It gave me more of an understanding,” she said of her reasoning for the research. “I was just trying to deep dive on all the reasons why they had derived so much pleasure from watching their woman be with other men.”

The witness theorized that Combs’ desire for “hotel nights,” the details of which are nearly identical to Ventura's “freak offs,” was because he may be curious about his own sexuality.

“Cucks could also have a real curiosity that they're too ashamed to experience themselves,” she testified, “and so they use the woman to venture out in this curiosity without actually doing the act itself.”

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Mentions Koby Bryant, Shaquille O’Neil and Michael Jordan During Testimony

Jane testified that she had nicknamed Paul—an escort she and Combs solicited on multiple occasions—“Trifecta” because she compared their sexual dynamic to the on-court success of basketball stars.

“I was Kobe Bryant and Paul was Shaq [O’Neil],” she told jurors. “Combs was Michael Jordan.”

She described Paul as “really nice,” noting that if she “had to” have sex with escorts, “at least it was with someone with a warm energy.”

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Says Mogul’s Chief of Staff “Influenced a Great Deal” of Their Romance

Jane testified that Combs’ dynamic with Khorram was a “point of contention” in the witness’ romance with the rap mogul.

“She was always with him,” Jane said of Khorram. “It was difficult for our relationship. I believe that she had very strong feelings about me, which influenced a great deal with how Sean treated me.”

Judge Denies Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Defense Team’s Second Motion for a Mistrial

On June 10, Judge Subramanian denied a motion for mistrial by Combs' legal team, in which his attorneys argued that Ventura and her friend Bryana Bongolan lied about the latter being dangled over the 17th-floor balcony by the rap mogul.

His lawyers, whose previous motion for a mistrial was also denied, accused the prosecution in the filing of “misconduct” with the “introduction of perjured testimony.”

“In this trial the government has presented testimony that it knew or should have known was materially false related to its allegation that Mr. Combs dangled Bryana Bongolan from the balcony of Cassie Ventura’s apartment in September 2016,” the letter said. “Accordingly, to avoid an unfair conviction in this case the Court should grant a mistrial.”

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Details Hourslong 2024 Beating

Jane alleged that the rap mogul had beaten her for hours June 2024.

Jane testified that she and Combs began arguing about the latter seeing a woman who was 25 years his junior, which Jane said led to her calling him a “pedophile” and pushing his head into a marble kitchen counter.

The witness said she had hidden in the primary bedroom and Combs began kicking the door open. (Photos of the cracked door were entered into evidence.) She alleged that while trying to run away, the Bad Boy Records founder kicked her in the back of the thigh, which made her fall.

“He put me in a chokehold on the ground, lifted me up, and I couldn't breathe, and I was on my tippy toes,” she told the prosecution. “I was just trying to get out of his grip.”

Jane said she ran out of the house and hid behind a wall outside for approximately two hours, hoping he would leave, but he did not. She said they subsequently returned to his home, where she curled up in the backyard to protect herself as Combs allegedly attacked her.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Says Attack By Rapper Left  Her “Golfball-sized” Welts

According to Jane, the altercation escalated after Combs allegedly punched her back. “He started punching my head,” she told the court. “He started kicking me. He started saying all kinds of things and just kept punching.”

She alleged that he told her that she was “trying to take him away from his kids and his family.”

“I said, ‘No, I'm not. Just leave me alone. Just leave. Please stop,’” she testified. “Then he just grabs me by my arm and my hair and just starts dragging me back to the house … by my hair, by my arm.”

After the alleged assault, Jane said she saw “two golfball-sized welts” on her forehead, as well as a black eye.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Details Homeland Security Raid at Her Home

Jane said that Homeland Security Investigated raided her home, which was paid for by Combs in March 2023, along with multiple other residences owned by the mogul. She said that agents came to her house and she called Combs’ head of security to tell him about the situation.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Says He Threatened To Release Sex Tapes

Jane alleged that the mogul threatened to release their sex tapes and show them to her baby’s father in December 2023.

As Jane and Combs' relationship deteriorated, she said he told her that he had “nothing to lose” by releasing the videos. She testified, “It was just back-to-back pressure phone calls.”

She texted Khorram about the interaction, which was entered into evidence. In the texts, Jane wrote that Combs was threatening to post the footage, in which she said she was “heavily drugged.”

She also wrote that she needed “time away from” Combs because she was trying to work through “all the trauma” he caused.

Jane told the court that Khorram assured her that Combs would not use the footage.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Says She Fainted After Reading Cassie Ventura’s Lawsuit

Jane said during her testimony that parts of Ventura's November 2023 lawsuit—which was ultimately settled—mirrored her own experience with Combs.

“I almost fainted, in fact, I think I did,” she told jurors as she cried. “There was three specific  pages that was just a harrowing reference to what I was experiencing.”

Jane, who took part in “hotel nights, "said that it felt like she was reading “her own story.” It led to her confronting Combs via text messages.

“I feel like I am reading my own sexual trauma,” she wrote after Ventura’s lawsuit, in screenshots shown in evidence. “I am sick. It's exactly word for word, drug-filled days and nights. You knew this was coming. You gaslit me, you made me go crazy.”

“I am disgusted, I felt forced to perform back to back,” the messages continued. “You made me feel crazy about the sex trauma I was feeling. I feel very violated. This was sexual exploitation.”

Combs subsequently called Jane and recorded the conversation without her knowledge that was entered into evidence, in which he told her that they “did these things together” and that “this is when” he needed her “to be there.”

Jane, who didn’t know she was being recorded, told Combs that she was “sick” to her stomach after reading Ventura’s documents.

Sean “Diddy” Combs’ Ex Says She Threw Up 3 Times During “Hotel Night” Without Drugs

Jane said that while she was often high on ecstasy while participating in “Hotel Nights” with Combs and male escorts, one evening she decided not to take any drugs while having sex with three men.

“I was with multiple men this night,” she testified. “I threw up. Sean came in and said, ‘You’ll feel better now. Let’s go outside.’”

Jane said that at the end of the night, she felt “absolutely terrible.”

Treasury yields fall as investors crowd into bonds for safety amid election stock sell-off

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Treasury yields moved lower on Wednesday as investors sought shelter from a sell-off in equity futures sparked by potential big victories from Democrats in the U.S. midterm elections.

The benchmark 10-year Treasury note yield was last seen 2 basis points lower at 4.9291%. The 30-year bond yield slid 1 basis point to 5.3093%, and the shorter-term 2-year note yield pulled back 1 basis point to 4.5996%.

One basis point equals 0.01%, or 1/100th of 1%, and yields and prices move inversely to one another.

Democrats are on course for a clean sweep, with the party winning at least the House and projected to win the Senate according to a CNBC Election Desk.

The Associated Press is projecting that Democrats have flipped Senate seats from Republicans in Iowa, Kansas, North Carolina, Ohio, South Carolina, Maine and Texas.

Stock futures fell sharply as election results rolled in. Contracts tied to the S&P 500 were seen 0.17% lower, and Nasdaq-100 futures slid 0.25%.

AI-related shares, which have led the bull market, paced the overnight decline on fears that Democrats would slow the data center buildout.

The Barbie Diwali doll commemorates the annual celebration popular in Indian culture and known worldwide as the “Festival of Lights” (the word Diwali means “row of lights” in Hindi). She described Kennedy as the âbrain worm guyâ and slammed Trumpâs Cabinet as uniquely unsuited to their positions.Â

Harvey to Bring Jus Mundi’s Arbitration Agent Into Legal Workflows

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Harvey announced on November 4, 2026 a partnership to integrate Jus Mundi’s Jus AI arbitration agent into its Assistant, Agents, Vault and Workflows products. The integration is intended for international arbitration and cross-border disputes teams, with global availability to joint customers planned by the end of 2026 at no additional cost.

The integration is designed to place specialist research beside the matter record. Once activated, a lawyer evaluating a client’s position could draw on applicable awards, treaty practice and institutional rules, then test the resulting research against documents in the matter’s Harvey Vault. The matter remains in Harvey, and findings can be carried into a memo, pleading or witness-preparation outline. Each authority links back to its source document on Jus Mundi’s platform.

Those results can be reviewed in the context of the matter’s pleadings, exhibits and transcripts. Pinpoint citations support checking the underlying material before an answer is presented to a tribunal. Recurring disputes work covered by the integration includes preparing a Request for Arbitration, checking authorities while drafting, comparing exhibit bundles with precedent and identifying clauses that rely on superseded international rules.

Jus Mundi’s source base spans investment and commercial awards, decisions, treaties, procedural materials and publications. Content relationships include the IBA and arbitral institutions including the ICC, AAA-ICDR, LCIA, SIAC, HKIAC, DIAC and Court of Arbitration for Sport. Secondary materials include the ICC Dispute Resolution Bulletin, ICSID Review and Redfern and Hunter on International Arbitration.

Jus Mundi’s Jus AI product page says Jus Mundi Data contains more than 125,000 case documents, awards and leading publications through more than 110 exclusive institutional and publisher partnerships. Its My Sources feature can search and analyze up to 10,000 user documents, with files secured and encrypted in a private Vault.

Jus AI runs on Jus AI Tenet V5, which Jus Mundi describes as a proprietary large language model trained on arbitration and international law. Users can choose four reasoning modes — Auto, Light, Think and Deep — and inspect reasoning steps, check citations and follow links back to sources.

The integration will operate within Harvey’s existing security, confidentiality and data-governance controls. Jus Mundi separately lists ISO 27001 and ISO 42001 certifications and a SOC 2 Type II attestation for Jus AI. The security provisions are central to a workflow that combines outside research with confidential case materials and private user documents.

Harvey Chief Business Officer John Haddock said the partnership responds to clients seeking deep domain expertise inside the platform where they manage important matters. Emmanuel Jacomy, a partner at A&O Shearman, said bringing the tools together would give arbitration teams a new way of working, with more time for client advocacy and strategic tasks.

For arbitration teams, the key change is where the work happens: source research, citation checking and drafting are designed to share one matter context, reducing the handoffs between a specialist research platform and the case file.

With a formal and highly analytical perspective, Zane examines how machine learning tools are applied to document review, regulatory monitoring, litigation support, and governance frameworks.
